<h2 id="decocms-blocks-cli">`@decocms/blocks-cli`</h2>

| Import path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `@decocms/blocks-cli/generate` | The `generate` orchestrator. Run it as `tsx node_modules/@decocms/blocks-cli/scripts/generate.ts`. |
| `@decocms/blocks-cli/generate-blocks` | `generateBlocks` and `readBlockDelta`, used by the TanStack Vite plugin. |

Commands (`bin`): `deco-migrate`, `deco-post-cleanup`, `deco-htmx-analyze`, `deco-reconcile`, `deco-upgrade-6-to-7`, `deco-sync-blocks-to-kv`, `deco-migrate-blocks-to-kv`, `deco-sync-blocks-bot`, `deco-cf-observability`, `deco-audit-observability`. Run them with `npx -p @decocms/blocks-cli <command>`. The individual generator scripts and `audit-secrets.ts`, `cdn-rules.ts` and `tailwind-lint.ts` are reachable only by file path under `node_modules/@decocms/blocks-cli/scripts/`. See [CLI reference](/storefront/blocks/v7/cli).

<h2 id="decocms-tanstack">`@decocms/tanstack`</h2>

| Import path | What it is | Runs on |
|---|---|---|
| `@decocms/tanstack` | Route configs (`cmsRouteConfig`, `cmsHomeRouteConfig`, `decoMetaRouteConfig`, `decoRenderRouteConfig`, `decoInvokeRouteConfig`), server functions (`loadCmsPage`, `loadCmsHomePage`, `loadDeferredSection`), components (`DecoRootLayout`, `DecoPageRenderer`, `SectionRenderer`, `SectionList`, `NavigationProgress`, `StableOutlet`, `DraftPreviewIndicator`, `PreviewProviders`, `CmsPage`, `NotFoundPage`), `createDecoWorkerEntry`, `setupTanstackFastDeploy`, `createDecoRouter`, speculation rules. | both |
| `@decocms/tanstack/vite` | `decoVitePlugin`. Plain JavaScript without type declarations: add `// @ts-expect-error` above the import in a TypeScript config. | build |
| `@decocms/tanstack/sdk/deferredSectionLoader` | `deferredSectionLoader`, for `DecoPageRenderer`'s `loadDeferredSectionFn`. | both |
| `@decocms/tanstack/sdk/serverFnFetch` | `decoServerFnFetch`, for your own `src/start.ts`. | browser |
| `@decocms/tanstack/sdk/startEntry` | The default TanStack Start entry used when your site has no `src/start.ts`. | both |
| `@decocms/tanstack/sdk/cookiePassthrough` | `getRequestCookieHeader`, `forwardResponseCookies`. | server |
| `@decocms/tanstack/sdk/cdnSegment` | Constants of the CDN segment marker. Framework plumbing. | both |
| `@decocms/tanstack/sdk/createInvoke` | A type marker read by `generate` when it writes `invoke.gen.ts`. It throws if called. | build |
| `@decocms/tanstack/daemon` | The local development tunnel to Studio, started by the Vite plugin. Not for direct use. | dev |

The option types of `createDecoWorkerEntry` aren't exported; their shapes are written out in [TanStack Start on Cloudflare Workers](/storefront/blocks/v7/tanstack).

<h2 id="decocms-nextjs">`@decocms/nextjs`</h2>

| Import path | What it is | Runs on |
|---|---|---|
| `@decocms/nextjs` | `createDecoPage`, `createDecoPreviewPage`, `DecoRootLayout`, `DecoPageRenderer`, `SectionRenderer`, draft preview helpers (`ensureDraft`, `DraftPreviewIndicator`, …). For `page.tsx` and `layout.tsx`, **never** `route.ts`. | server components |
| `@decocms/nextjs/routeHandlers` | `createDecoRouteHandlers`. The import for `route.ts` files. | server |
| `@decocms/nextjs/setup` | `createNextSetup`. | server |
| `@decocms/nextjs/config` | `withDeco`, `DECO_REWRITES`. CommonJS, so it works from `next.config.js` and `next.config.ts`. | build |
| `@decocms/nextjs/middleware` | `draftMiddleware`, `prepareDraft`, `applyDraft`, `draftRequestHeaders`, `rewriteToDraftRoute`. | edge middleware |

<h2 id="decocms-eitri">`@decocms/eitri`</h2>

| Import path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `@decocms/eitri` | `generateEitri`, `eitriGenerateArgs`, `runEitriInit`. |
| `@decocms/eitri/tsconfig` | A base `tsconfig.json` to extend. |
| `@decocms/eitri/types` | Type shims for the Eitri runtime modules. |

Command: `deco-eitri` (`init`, `generate`). See [Eitri apps](/storefront/blocks/v7/eitri).

<h2 id="apps">Apps</h2>

Wildcard paths (`*`) map to the files of that directory, without the extension.

<h3 id="decocms-apps-commerce">`@decocms/apps-commerce`</h3>

`./types` (also the package's main entry), `./types/cart`, `./app-types`, `./resolve`, `./manifest-utils`, `./registry`, `./sdk/*` (`formatPrice`, `url`, `useOffer`, `useVariantPossibilities`, `analytics`), `./utils/*` (`filters`, `canonical`, `productToAnalyticsItem`, `constants`, `stateByZip`). There's no root import; use `@decocms/apps-commerce/types`. See [Commerce types and utilities](/storefront/blocks/v7/apps-commerce).

<h3 id="decocms-apps-website">`@decocms/apps-website`</h3>

`.`, `./mod`, `./client`, `./types`, `./components/*` (`Seo`, `Analytics`, `OneDollarStats`, `Stats`, `Theme`, `Video`), `./loaders/*`, `./loaders/fonts/*`, `./utils/*`, `./sections/*`, `./sections/Seo/*`. No `./registry`. See [Website app](/storefront/blocks/v7/apps-website).

<h3 id="decocms-apps-vtex">`@decocms/apps-vtex`</h3>

`.`, `./client`, `./mod`, `./registry`, `./commerceLoaders`, `./schemas`, `./types`, `./middleware`, `./actions`, `./actions/*`, `./actions/analytics/*`, `./loaders`, `./loaders/*`, `./loaders/intelligentSearch/*`, `./loaders/legacy/*`, `./loaders/workflow/*`, `./inline-loaders/productDetailsPage`, `./inline-loaders/productListingPage`, `./inline-loaders/productList`, `./inline-loaders/productListShelf`, `./inline-loaders/relatedProducts`, `./inline-loaders/suggestions`, `./inline-loaders/minicart`, `./inline-loaders/workflowProducts`, `./hooks`, `./hooks/*`, `./utils`, `./utils/*`. Extra optional peer: `@tanstack/react-query` ≥5 for the Query-based hooks. See [VTEX](/storefront/blocks/v7/vtex).

<h3 id="decocms-apps-shopify">`@decocms/apps-shopify`</h3>

`.`, `./client`, `./mod`, `./registry`, `./loaders/*`, `./actions/*`, `./actions/cart/*`, `./actions/user/*`, `./utils/*`. See [Shopify](/storefront/blocks/v7/shopify).

<h3 id="decocms-apps-wake">`@decocms/apps-wake`</h3>

`.`, `./client`, `./mod`, `./registry`, `./commerceLoaders`, `./loaders/*`, `./actions/*`, `./actions/cart/*`, `./actions/newsletter/*`, `./actions/review/*`, `./actions/wishlist/*`, `./handlers/*`, `./utils/*`. The map also declares `./hooks` and `./hooks/*`, but the package ships no hook files, so those paths don't resolve. See [Wake](/storefront/blocks/v7/wake).

<h3 id="decocms-apps-magento">`@decocms/apps-magento`</h3>

`.`, `./client`, `./types`, `./middleware`, `./loaders/*`, `./actions/*`, `./utils/*`. No `mod` or `registry`. The map declares `./hooks/*`, but the package ships no hook files, so that path doesn't resolve. See [Magento](/storefront/blocks/v7/magento).

<h3 id="decocms-apps-salesforce">`@decocms/apps-salesforce`</h3>

<h3 id="decocms-apps-algolia">`@decocms/apps-algolia`</h3>

`.`, `./client`, `./types`, `./loaders/*`. Extra peer: `algoliasearch` ^5, required by `.` and `./client`. See [Algolia](/storefront/blocks/v7/algolia).

<h3 id="decocms-apps-blog">`@decocms/apps-blog`</h3>

`.`, `./mod`, `./client`, `./registry`, `./types`, `./manifest.gen`, `./loaderMap`, `./loaders/*`, `./loaders/extensions/*`, `./actions/*`, `./core/*`, `./utils/*`, `./sections/*`, `./sections/Seo/*`, `./sections/blocks/*`, `./static/*`. See [Blog](/storefront/blocks/v7/blog).

Document: Configuration reference

# Configuration reference

> Every environment variable, binding and setup option a v7 site reads, in one place.

<Callout type="note">**Current Site Editor workflow.** Studio saves repository-backed content edits to a working branch. **Publish** pushes and synchronizes that branch, opens or updates a pull request, and squash-merges it; **Request review** leaves the PR unmerged. The deployment integration applies the merged content. The runtime `POST /.decofile` endpoint below is a separate v7 capability for clients and delivery integrations, not the current Studio Publish action. See [connecting a site](/storefront/editor/connect-site) and [publishing changes](/storefront/editor/content/releases).</Callout>

This page collects every environment variable and Cloudflare binding v7 reads, and the options of the setup functions, route configs and Worker entry. Each table links back to the page that explains the feature. On Cloudflare Workers, set variables under `vars` in `wrangler.jsonc` (or as secrets for sensitive values); on Next.js and in Node tooling, set them in the process environment.

<h2 id="environment-variables-and-bindings">Environment variables and bindings</h2>

<h3 id="runtime">Runtime</h3>

| Name | Read by | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `NODE_ENV` | all packages | | `development` turns on dev behaviour: no loader cache, no auth on `POST /.decofile`, stack traces in invoke errors, observability off. |
| `DECO_PREVIEW` | `@decocms/blocks` | | `true` makes `isDevMode()` return true outside development. |
| `DECO_SITE_NAME` | `@decocms/tanstack`, observability, Vite plugin | | The site's name: infers its Deco-hosted preview hosts, names the service in telemetry, and is passed to `generate --site` in development. |
| `DECO_SITE` | `@decocms/blocks/middleware` | `storefront` | The site name in `buildDecoState`. |
| `DECO_CRYPTO_KEY` | `@decocms/blocks/sdk/crypto` | | Key that decrypts secrets stored encrypted in the decofile (app credentials). A secret. See [Apps](/storefront/blocks/v7/apps). |

<h3 id="studio-and-the-admin-protocol">Studio and the admin protocol</h3>

| Name | Read by | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DECO_RELEASE_RELOAD_TOKEN` | `@decocms/blocks-admin` | | Required for `POST /.decofile` outside development: the `Authorization` header must equal it exactly. Without it, runtime reload requests get 401; this is separate from Studio's GitHub publication. See [Site Editor and the v7 admin protocol](/storefront/blocks/v7/studio). |

<h3 id="draft-preview">Draft preview</h3>

| Name | Read by | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DECO_ALLOWED_PREVIEW_HOSTS` | `@decocms/blocks` | the Site block's `previewHosts` | Comma-separated request hosts (with port) allowed to render drafts. Replaces the Site block's list. `none` turns draft preview off. |
| `DECO_PREVIEW_API_DOMAINS` | `@decocms/blocks` | Studio's domains and `localhost` | Comma-separated domains drafts may be fetched from. A leading `.` matches subdomains. |

See [Previews and draft preview](/storefront/blocks/v7/preview).

<h3 id="fast-deploy-tanstack">Fast Deploy (TanStack)</h3>

| Name | Kind | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DECO_FAST_DEPLOY` | variable | | `1` or `true` turns Fast Deploy on, together with `DECO_KV`. |
| `DECO_KV` | KV binding | | The namespace holding content snapshots. |
| `DECO_DEPLOYMENT_ID` | variable | `BUILD_HASH`, then the build-time hash | The deployment whose snapshot this Worker reads and writes. Passed per deploy. |
| `DECO_SEEDED_DEPLOY` | build variable | | Set by deployment pipelines that seed KV before activation; makes `decoVitePlugin` stub bundled content out in `"auto"` mode. |
| `CF_ACCOUNT_ID`, `CF_API_TOKEN`, `CF_KV_NAMESPACE_ID` | CLI variables | `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, the namespace in `wrangler.jsonc` | Credentials for `deco-sync-blocks-to-kv` and `deco-migrate-blocks-to-kv`. |

See [Deploying and Fast Deploy](/storefront/blocks/v7/releases).

<h3 id="caching">Caching</h3>

| Name | Read by | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BUILD_HASH` | `@decocms/tanstack` | the build-time hash | Version added to every edge cache key. Rename with `cacheVersionEnv`. |
| `PURGE_TOKEN` | `@decocms/tanstack` | | Bearer token for `POST /_cache/purge` and `/_cache/purge-loaders`. Rename with `purgeTokenEnv`. |
| `DECO_LOADER_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` | `@decocms/blocks` | 32 MB | Size cap of the in-memory loader cache. |
| `DECO_CACHE_DISABLE` | `@decocms/blocks` | | `true` disables the loader cache and shortens route cache times. |

See [Caching](/storefront/blocks/v7/caching).

<h3 id="observability">Observability</h3>

| Name | Kind | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DECO_OTEL` | variable | auto | `on`, `off`, or unset (on when deployed, off in development). |
| `DECO_OTEL_TRACES_ENDPOINT` | variable | | Collector endpoint for spans (OTLP/HTTP). |
| `DECO_OTEL_METRICS_ENDPOINT` | variable | | Collector endpoint for metrics. |
| `DECO_OTEL_LOGS_ENDPOINT` | variable | | Collector endpoint for logs. |
| `DECO_OTEL_HEADERS` | variable | | Extra OTLP headers, `k=v,k2=v2`. |
| `DECO_OTEL_AUTH_TOKEN` | secret | | `Authorization` header value for the collector. |
| `DECO_OTEL_TRACES_SAMPLING_RATE` | variable | `0.01` | Fraction of traces exported. |
| `DECO_OTEL_LOGS_MIN_LEVEL` | variable | `info` | Lowest log level posted. |
| `DECO_OTEL_ERROR_PROMOTION`, `DECO_OTEL_ERROR_PROMOTION_RATE` | variables | off, `0.1` | Export a share of unsampled error traces. |
| `DECO_ENV_NAME` | variable | `production` | `deployment.environment` on telemetry. |
| `DECO_METRICS` | Analytics Engine binding | | Metrics to Analytics Engine. |
| `CF_VERSION_METADATA` | `version_metadata` binding | | `service.version` on telemetry. |
| `OTEL_LOG_OUTGOING_FETCH` | variable | | `true` logs every outgoing fetch. |

See [Observability](/storefront/blocks/v7/observability).

<h3 id="analytics">Analytics</h3>

| Name | Read by | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DECO_ANALYTICS_ENABLED` | `Stats` (`@decocms/blocks/hooks`) | | Must be exactly `true` for the first-party analytics tag to render. |
| `DECO_ANALYTICS_ORIGIN` | `Stats` | same origin | Origin the tag loads from. |
| `DECO_ANALYTICS_SITE_KEY` | `Stats` | | Site key, for sites not served through Deco's edge. |
| `ONEDOLLAR_ENABLED` | `OneDollarStats` (`@decocms/apps-website`) | enabled | `false` disables it. |
| `ONEDOLLAR_COLLECTOR`, `ONEDOLLAR_STATIC_SCRIPT` | `OneDollarStats` | | Collector and script URL overrides. |

<h3 id="apps">Apps</h3>

| Name | App | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `VTEX_APP_KEY`, `VTEX_APP_TOKEN` | VTEX | Fallback credentials when the `deco-vtex` block has none. |
| `VTEX_RESILIENCE_DISABLED` | VTEX | `true` turns off retries and the circuit breaker in `createVtexFetch`. |
| `SHOPIFY_STOREFRONT_TOKEN` | Shopify | Fallback Storefront API token. |
| `WAKE_TOKEN` | Wake | The Storefront API token. Required; Wake reads it only from the environment. |
| `RESEND_API_KEY` | Resend | Fallback API key. |

Apps whose credentials are stored as encrypted secrets also need `DECO_CRYPTO_KEY`. See each app's page under [Apps](/storefront/blocks/v7/apps).

<h3 id="development">Development</h3>

| Name | Read by | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `DECO_SITE_NAME` + `DECO_ENV_NAME` | Vite plugin | When both are set in `vite dev`, the plugin starts the v7 tunnel registered with legacy `admin.deco.cx`; it does not import a repository into Studio. |
| `DECO_HOST` | Vite plugin | `false` selects the older tunnel relay. |
| `WORKERS_CI_COMMIT_SHA` | Vite plugin | Used as the build hash on Cloudflare Workers Builds. |

<h2 id="a-complete-wrangler-jsonc">A complete `wrangler.jsonc`</h2>

```jsonc title="wrangler.jsonc"
{
  "name": "my-store",
  "main": "src/worker-entry.ts",
  "compatibility_date": "2026-02-14",
  "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat", "no_handle_cross_request_promise_resolution"],
  "kv_namespaces": [{ "binding": "DECO_KV", "id": "<your namespace id>" }],
  "vars": {
    "DECO_SITE_NAME": "my-store",
    "DECO_ENV_NAME": "production",
    "DECO_FAST_DEPLOY": "1"
  },
  "observability": {
    "enabled": true,
    "logs": { "enabled": true, "head_sampling_rate": 1 },
    "traces": { "enabled": true, "head_sampling_rate": 0.01 }
  },
  "version_metadata": { "binding": "CF_VERSION_METADATA" }
}
```

- `nodejs_compat` is required: request context uses `AsyncLocalStorage`.
- `no_handle_cross_request_promise_resolution` lets the framework's caches share an in-flight request across concurrent visitors; without it, the Worker can hang.
- Set `DECO_RELEASE_RELOAD_TOKEN`, `PURGE_TOKEN`, `DECO_CRYPTO_KEY` and `DECO_OTEL_AUTH_TOKEN` as secrets (`wrangler secret put`), not in `vars`.

<h2 id="createsitesetup">`createSiteSetup`</h2>

From `@decocms/blocks/setup`. See [Blocks and sections](/storefront/blocks/v7/model).

| Option | Type | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sections` | `Record<string, () => Promise<any>>` | required | Section modules keyed `./sections/<path>.tsx`, as `import.meta.glob` returns them. Registered as `site/sections/<path>.tsx`. |
| `blocks` | `Record<string, unknown>` | required | The decofile, usually `blocks` from `.deco/blocks.gen`. |
| `productionOrigins` | `string[]` | | Absolute URLs on these origins in content are made relative. |
| `customMatchers` | `Array<() => void>` | | Functions that register your own matchers. Built-in matchers are always registered. |
| `onResolveError` | `(error, resolveType, context) => void` | | Called when a loader or section fails during resolution. |
| `onDanglingReference` | `(resolveType) => any` | warns, returns `null` | Called for a loader or action key nothing registered. |
| `initPlatform` | `(blocks) => void` | | Runs with the content at setup and again whenever it changes, to configure a platform. |

<h2 id="createadminsetup">`createAdminSetup`</h2>

From `@decocms/blocks-admin/setup`. TanStack only; Next.js passes these to `createNextSetup`. See [Site Editor and the v7 admin protocol](/storefront/blocks/v7/studio).

| Option | Type | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `meta` | `() => Promise<any>` | required | Loads the schema lazily, usually `() => import("../.deco/meta.gen.json").then((m) => m.default)`. |
| `css` | `string` | required | URL of your stylesheet for preview pages, from a `?url` import. |
| `fonts` | `string[]` | `[]` | Font stylesheet URLs for previews. |
| `previewWrapper` | `React.ComponentType` | | Wraps every preview, to provide context (TanStack: `PreviewProviders`). |
| `getCommerceLoaders` | `() => Record<string, (props, request?) => Promise<any>>` | | Loaders made invokable at `/deco/invoke`. |

For a preview theme, body class or language, call `setRenderShell({ theme: "light", bodyClass, lang })` from `@decocms/blocks-admin`.

<h2 id="createnextsetup">`createNextSetup`</h2>

From `@decocms/nextjs/setup`. Returns `ensureSetup()`. See [Next.js App Router](/storefront/blocks/v7/nextjs).

| Option | Type | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sections` | `Record<string, () => Promise<any>>` | required | Section modules keyed `./sections/<path>.tsx`; use the generated `sectionImports`. |
| `blocks` | `Record<string, unknown>` | | Content, usually the generated block manifest. Merged over `blocksDir`. |
| `blocksDir` | `string \| false` | `".deco/blocks"` | Directory read at runtime. `false` when you pass `blocks`. |
| `conventions` | `{ meta, syncComponents, loadingFallbacks }` | | Section conventions from `.deco/sections.gen.ts`. |
| `meta` | `() => Promise<unknown>` | | The schema. Without it, `/live/_meta` returns 503. |
| `renderShell` | `{ css?: string; fonts?: string[] }` | | Preview stylesheet and fonts. |
| `previewWrapper` | `React.ComponentType` | | Wraps previews. |
| `productionOrigins`, `customMatchers`, `onResolveError`, `onDanglingReference` | | | As in `createSiteSetup`. |
| `extend` | `(blocks) => void \| Promise<void>` | | Runs last, with the loaded content. |

<h2 id="cmsrouteconfig-and-cmshomerouteconfig">`cmsRouteConfig` and `cmsHomeRouteConfig`</h2>

From `@decocms/tanstack`. Spread into `createFileRoute("/$")` and `createFileRoute("/")`. See [TanStack Start on Cloudflare Workers](/storefront/blocks/v7/tanstack).

| Option | Type | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `siteName` | `string` | required (`/$`); `defaultTitle` (`/`) | Used in page titles. |
| `defaultTitle` | `string` | required | Title when a page has none. |
| `defaultDescription` | `string` | | Description when a page has none. |
| `ignoreSearchParams` | `string[]` | `["skuId"]` | Query parameters that don't trigger a reload. `/$` only. |
| `pendingComponent` | component | none | Shown during slow navigations. Without it, the previous page stays visible. |
| `pendingMs`, `pendingMinMs` | `number` | `200`, `300` | When the pending component shows, and for how long at least. |
| `errorComponent` | component | built-in error page | Shown when loading fails. |
| `ssr` | `boolean \| "data-only"` | full SSR | TanStack Start's SSR mode. `/$` only. |
| `resolveGlobals` | `boolean` | `true` | Merge the Site block's global sections and theme into every page. |

<h2 id="createdecoworkerentry">`createDecoWorkerEntry`</h2>

From `@decocms/tanstack`: `createDecoWorkerEntry(serverEntry, options)`.

| Option | Type | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `admin` | `{ handleMeta, handleDecofileRead, handleDecofileReload, handleRender, corsHeaders }` | | Admin protocol handlers from `@decocms/blocks-admin`. Without it, `/live/_meta`, `/.decofile` and `/live/previews` aren't served. |
| `buildSegment` | `(request) => SegmentKey` | | The visitor's cache segment. See [Caching](/storefront/blocks/v7/caching#personalized-pages-segments). |
| `detectProfile` | `(url) => CacheProfileName \| null` | built-in rules | Choose a cache profile per URL. |
| `deviceSpecificKeys` | `boolean` | `true` | Split the cache by mobile/desktop when there's no `buildSegment`. |
| `geoCacheKey` | `"auto" \| "off" \| "country" \| "region" \| "city"` | `"auto"` | Geography in the cache key. |
| `autoInjectGeoCookies` | `boolean` | `true` | Expose Cloudflare geolocation to matchers. |
| `safeCookies` | `string[]` | VTEX session cookies and `_deco_bucket` | Cookies that don't prevent caching. |
| `stripTrackingParams` | `boolean` | `true` | Remove tracking parameters from cache keys. |
| `bypassPaths` | `string[]` | `/_build`, `/deco/`, `/live/`, `/.decofile` | Never cached. Your entries are added to the defaults. |
| `extraBypassPaths` | `string[]` | `[]` | More paths never cached. |
| `staticPaths` | `string[]` | `["/fonts/"]` | Path prefixes of non-fingerprinted files (fonts, icons) that get long-lived immutable cache headers. |
| `fingerprintedAssetPattern` | `RegExp` | hashed files under `/assets/` | Assets cached for a year. |
| `cacheVersionEnv` | `string \| false` | `"BUILD_HASH"` | Variable with the deploy version for cache keys. |
| `purgeTokenEnv` | `string \| false` | `"PURGE_TOKEN"` | Variable with the purge token. `false` disables purging. |
| `cacheStorage` | `(env, request) => CacheStorage \| null` | Cache API + memory | Shared cache storage. |
| `cdnCacheControl` | `"serverfn-segment" \| "no-store" \| "match-profile" \| (profile) => string \| null` | `"serverfn-segment"` | `CDN-Cache-Control` policy. |
| `renderJson` | `boolean` | `true` | Serve `?renderJson`. See [Storefront as an API](/storefront/blocks/v7/storefront-api). |
| `asJson` | `boolean` | `true` | Serve `?asJson`. |
| `pageJsonCors` | `string[] \| "*" \| false` | `"*"` | CORS for page JSON. |
| `proxyHandler` | `(request, url) => Response \| null \| Promise<…>` | | Proxy paths to another origin (checkout, for example). |
| `previewShell` | `string` | built from the render shell | HTML for the empty preview frame. |
| `securityHeaders` | `Record<string, string> \| false` | `nosniff`, HSTS, referrer and permissions policies, `frame-ancestors` for Studio | Headers on HTML responses. |
| `csp` | `string[] \| false` | | Content Security Policy directives. |
| `cspMode` | `"report-only" \| "enforce"` | `"report-only"` | `enforce` adds a per-request nonce on uncached HTML. |
| `speculationRules` | `SpeculationRulesConfig` | off | See [Speculation rules](/storefront/blocks/v7/speculation-rules). |
| `observability` | `OtelOptions \| false` | on | See [Observability](/storefront/blocks/v7/observability). |
| `outboundUserAgent` | `string \| false` | `Deco/<version> (+https://deco.cx)` | `User-Agent` added to outgoing `fetch` calls that have none. |

<h2 id="decoviteplugin">`decoVitePlugin`</h2>

From `@decocms/tanstack/vite`.

| Option | Type | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fastDeploy` | `boolean \| "auto"` | `"auto"` | Remove the bundled content from the server bundle (production builds only). `"auto"` does so only when `DECO_SEEDED_DEPLOY` is set. See [Deploying and Fast Deploy](/storefront/blocks/v7/releases#bundle-stub-mode-advanced). |

<h2 id="related">Related</h2>

- [Packages and exports](/storefront/blocks/v7/packages)
- [CLI reference](/storefront/blocks/v7/cli): command flags.


For current Studio onboarding, use [Connect a site](/storefront/editor/connect-site). The built-in v7 tunnel's legacy destination is retained here as an implementation reference, not recommended onboarding for the discontinued admin.

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/blocks/v7/glossary
Document: Glossary

# Glossary

> The terms used throughout the v7 docs, each with a short definition and a pointer to where it's explained.

The words these docs use for Deco Blocks v7, in alphabetical order. Each entry is a short definition with a link to the page that explains it in full.

<h2 id="action">Action</h2>

A server function that changes something, such as adding an item to a cart or sending an email. Actions are called by key through [invoke](#invoke). See [Loaders and actions](/storefront/blocks/v7/loaders).

<h2 id="admin-protocol">Admin protocol</h2>

The HTTP endpoints compatible v7 editor and delivery clients can call on your running site: `GET /live/_meta` (the [schema](#schema-meta)), `GET` and `POST /.decofile` (read and reload runtime content), `/live/previews/*` and `/deco/render` (previews) and `/deco/invoke` (run loaders and actions). Implemented by `@decocms/blocks-admin` and mounted by each [binding](#binding). See [Site Editor and the v7 admin protocol](/storefront/blocks/v7/studio).

<h2 id="app">App</h2>

A companion package, `@decocms/apps-*`, that brings the loaders, actions, sections and configuration for one platform (VTEX, Shopify, a blog, an email provider). An app is configured from a [block](#block) in the [decofile](#decofile), such as `deco-vtex`. See [Apps](/storefront/blocks/v7/apps).

<h2 id="binding">Binding</h2>

The framework package that connects the runtime to an app framework: `@decocms/tanstack` for TanStack Start on Cloudflare Workers, or `@decocms/nextjs` for the Next.js App Router. The binding finds the page for a URL, renders it, and mounts the [admin protocol](#admin-protocol). See [How v7 works](/storefront/blocks/v7/architecture).

<h2 id="block">Block</h2>

A named, typed piece of JSON content in the [decofile](#decofile) that the runtime can resolve: a [section](#section), a [page](#page-block), a [loader](#loader) call, a [matcher](#matcher), an [app](#app) configuration. A block's type is given by its [`__resolveType`](#resolvetype). See [Blocks and sections](/storefront/blocks/v7/model).

<h2 id="bucket">Bucket</h2>

During a migration traffic split, the site that serves a visitor: `worker` (the new site) or `fallback` (the original). See [Going live after a migration](/storefront/blocks/v7/migration-cutover#shift-traffic-gradually).

<h2 id="cache-profile">Cache profile</h2>

A named caching policy, one of `static`, `product`, `listing`, `search`, `cart`, `private` and `none`, applied consistently at the edge, browser, loader and client layers. The framework picks one from the URL unless you choose. See [Caching](/storefront/blocks/v7/caching).

<h2 id="commerce-loader">Commerce loader</h2>

A [loader](#loader) referenced from content by key, such as `vtex/loaders/intelligentSearch/productListingPage.ts`, and registered at setup with `registerCommerceLoaders` (apps provide ready-made maps). The resolver calls it when it meets that key and passes the page URL along. See [Loaders and actions](/storefront/blocks/v7/loaders).

<h2 id="deco-blocks">Deco Blocks</h2>

The framework these docs describe. "v7" means the current released version of its packages: `@decocms/blocks`, `@decocms/blocks-admin`, `@decocms/blocks-cli`, `@decocms/tanstack` and `@decocms/nextjs`. See [Deco Blocks v7](/storefront/blocks/v7/overview).

<h2 id="deco-studio">Deco Studio</h2>

Deco's extensible platform for organizing and executing work. Its Site Editor app provides the storefront forms and previews. The current Site Editor reads metadata and renders site previews while saving repository-backed edits to a working branch. Publish opens or updates a PR and merges it; the deployment integration applies the merged content. The v7 [admin protocol](#admin-protocol) separately supports runtime reads, previews, invoke and authorized content reloads. See [Site Editor and the v7 admin protocol](/storefront/blocks/v7/studio).

<h2 id="decofile">Decofile</h2>

The site's content: a flat map from [block](#block) name to JSON. It's stored as one file per block under `.deco/blocks/`, committed to your repository, and bundled as `.deco/blocks.gen.json` by `generate`. See [Content and the decofile](/storefront/blocks/v7/content).

<h2 id="deferred-section">Deferred section</h2>

A [section](#section) rendered first as a skeleton (its `LoadingFallback`) and loaded separately afterwards, when it nears the viewport. Editors mark a section deferred with the ⚡ toggle in Studio. Bots always get the full page. See [Deferred sections](/storefront/blocks/v7/rendering).

<h2 id="deployment-id">Deployment id</h2>

The identifier of one deployed version of your code, normally its git commit sha, passed as `DECO_DEPLOYMENT_ID`. [Fast Deploy](#fast-deploy) keys content by it so each code version reads its own content. See [Deploying and Fast Deploy](/storefront/blocks/v7/releases).

<h2 id="draft-preview">Draft preview</h2>

Rendering unpublished Studio content on the real site through a `?__draft=` link. It works only on allowed hosts, is never cached, and is marked `noindex`. `?__draft=off` leaves it. See [Previews and draft preview](/storefront/blocks/v7/preview).

<h2 id="edge-cache">Edge cache</h2>

The cache in front of your pages on TanStack Start: the [Worker entry](#worker-entry) stores whole rendered responses in Cloudflare's cache, per cache key, so repeat requests skip rendering. See [Caching](/storefront/blocks/v7/caching).

<h2 id="etag">ETag</h2>

A version tag sent with a response. `/live/_meta` sends a hash of the [schema](#schema-meta) and answers `304` when Studio's `If-None-Match` still matches, so Studio skips downloading an unchanged schema. `/.decofile` sends the content [revision](#revision). See [Site Editor and the v7 admin protocol](/storefront/blocks/v7/studio).

<h2 id="fast-deploy">Fast Deploy</h2>

An opt-in feature of `@decocms/tanstack` that serves content from Cloudflare KV, so compatible runtime or CI content updates propagate between Worker instances without a code deploy. This does not by itself configure Studio's publish delivery. It needs `DECO_FAST_DEPLOY=1`, a `DECO_KV` binding and `setupTanstackFastDeploy()`. See [Deploying and Fast Deploy](/storefront/blocks/v7/releases).

<h2 id="invoke">Invoke</h2>

Calling a [loader](#loader) or [action](#action) by key over HTTP, at `POST /deco/invoke/<key>`, or from code through the `invoke` proxy in `@decocms/blocks/sdk/invoke`. See [Loaders and actions](/storefront/blocks/v7/loaders).

<h2 id="island">Island</h2>

In Fresh, components hydrated one by one on an otherwise static page. v7 has no islands: it renders the page on the server and hydrates it with React. See [Migrating from Fresh and Deno](/storefront/blocks/v7/migrate-from-fresh).

<h2 id="layout-section">Layout section</h2>

A [section](#section), such as a header or footer, whose resolved output is cached for a few minutes and shared across pages, keyed by device. Register layout sections with `registerLayoutSections` or `export const layout = true`. A section whose output depends on cookies, location or query parameters shouldn't be one. See [Section conventions](/storefront/blocks/v7/sections).

<h2 id="live-controls">Live controls</h2>

`LiveControls`, the small script `DecoRootLayout` renders so Studio and a previewed page can talk to each other. See [Site Editor and the v7 admin protocol](/storefront/blocks/v7/studio#live-controls).

<h2 id="loader">Loader</h2>

A server function that fetches data. A loader is either called from content (a [commerce loader](#commerce-loader)) or attached to a section (a [section loader](#section-loader)), and site loaders can also be [invoked](#invoke) by key. See [Loaders and actions](/storefront/blocks/v7/loaders).

<h2 id="createcachedloader">createCachedLoader</h2>

The loader cache: `createCachedLoader`, which shares a [loader](#loader)'s results across requests in memory, keyed by its props. See [Loaders and actions](/storefront/blocks/v7/loaders#cache-a-loader).

<h2 id="matcher">Matcher</h2>

A rule `(rule, context) => boolean` that content uses to choose between [variants](#variant-multivariate-flag): by device, cookie, date, host, path, query string, location, user agent or a random traffic split. Built-in matchers are registered by `createSiteSetup`; add your own with `registerMatcher`. See [Matchers and variants](/storefront/blocks/v7/variants).

<h2 id="page-block">Page block</h2>

A [block](#block) with a `path` and a list of `sections`, whose name starts with `pages-` or whose `__resolveType` is `website/pages/Page.tsx`. The binding renders the page block whose `path` matches the URL. See [Pages and routing](/storefront/blocks/v7/routing).

<h2 id="resolution">Resolution</h2>

Turning raw [decofile](#decofile) JSON into props ready to render, by following each [`__resolveType`](#resolvetype) recursively: dereferencing named blocks, choosing variants, calling commerce loaders. See [Blocks and sections](/storefront/blocks/v7/model) and, in depth, [How resolution works](/storefront/blocks/v7/walkthrough).

<h2 id="resolvetype">`__resolveType`</h2>

The field that says what a JSON value in the decofile is: a [section](#section) key (`site/sections/Hero.tsx`), a loader key, a matcher key, or the name of another block to reuse. See [Blocks and sections](/storefront/blocks/v7/model).

<h2 id="revision">Revision</h2>

A hash of the whole [decofile](#decofile). It changes whenever any block changes, and is used to detect new content (by [Fast Deploy](#fast-deploy)) and as a cache key. See [Content and the decofile](/storefront/blocks/v7/content).

<h2 id="schema-meta">Schema (meta)</h2>

The JSON Schema of your sections, loaders and pages, generated from their TypeScript types into `.deco/meta.gen.json` and served at `/live/_meta`. Studio builds its forms from it. See [Schema generation](/storefront/blocks/v7/schema).

<h2 id="section">Section</h2>

A React component that editors can place on a page. Its key is `site/sections/<path>.tsx`, matching its file under `src/sections/`, and its props are described by an exported `Props` type. See [Blocks and sections](/storefront/blocks/v7/model).

<h2 id="section-loader">Section loader</h2>

A server function that runs after [resolution](#resolution) and before rendering, enriching one section's props: the section's own `loader` export, or one registered with `registerSectionLoaders`. See [Loaders and actions](/storefront/blocks/v7/loaders).

<h2 id="segment">Segment</h2>

The part of a visitor's identity that changes what a page looks like (device, logged in or not, sales channel, region), returned by the Worker entry's `buildSegment` and added to the edge cache key. See [Caching](/storefront/blocks/v7/caching).

<h2 id="stale-while-revalidate-swr">Stale-while-revalidate (SWR)</h2>

Serving a cached result that has expired while a fresh one loads in the background, so visitors don't wait for the refresh. See [Caching](/storefront/blocks/v7/caching).

<h2 id="studio-tunnel">Legacy admin tunnel</h2>

The v7 Vite plugin's development tunnel integration, which registers with the legacy `admin.deco.cx` service. It is not the current Studio repository-import setup. The [Vite plugin](#vite-plugin) starts it when `DECO_SITE_NAME` and `DECO_ENV_NAME` are both set. See [Configuration reference](/storefront/blocks/v7/configuration#development).

<h2 id="variant-multivariate-flag">Variant (multivariate flag)</h2>

A block holding several alternatives, each guarded by a [matcher](#matcher). The first alternative whose matcher matches is used; if none does, the block is dropped. Used for A/B tests, scheduled content and personalization. See [Matchers and variants](/storefront/blocks/v7/variants).

<h2 id="vite-plugin">Vite plugin</h2>

`decoVitePlugin()` from `@decocms/tanstack/vite`, which keeps server code out of the browser bundle and regenerates files in development. See [TanStack Start on Cloudflare Workers](/storefront/blocks/v7/tanstack#the-vite-plugin).

<h2 id="widget">Widget</h2>

A type from `@decocms/blocks/types/widgets`, such as `ImageWidget`, that tells the schema generator which Studio input to show for a prop. See [Schema generation](/storefront/blocks/v7/schema).

<h2 id="worker-entry">Worker entry</h2>

`src/worker-entry.ts`, the file that calls `createDecoWorkerEntry`. It's the outermost handler of every request on TanStack Start: it serves the [admin protocol](#admin-protocol), the [edge cache](#edge-cache) and redirects. See [TanStack Start on Cloudflare Workers](/storefront/blocks/v7/tanstack#the-worker-entry).

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/overview
Document: Site Editor

# Site Editor

> Edit storefront content in Studio with a preview and a Git-backed publishing workflow.

The **Site Editor** is the Storefront application inside Studio. Developers define the components, data functions, and editable fields; content teams compose pages and configure them while seeing the result.

Start with [Connect a site](/storefront/editor/connect-site), then [create a page](/storefront/editor/content/pages), [edit its sections](/storefront/editor/content/sections), and [publish your changes](/storefront/editor/content/releases).

Fresh and the former deco.cx admin are retired. Use released Blocks 7.x for the website runtime and Studio Site Editor for current content work.

## How editing works

Open your site project in Studio. **Preview** shows the site with its editing controls; **Content** lists pages and saved configuration; **Code** supports development work. Available views depend on the project configuration and access.

Content forms come from the site's schemas. A field or component appears when the site exposes it. Changes autosave into the working branch; saving a draft and publishing are separate steps.

For content work, **Fast Preview** renders drafts using a configured preview server. Coding sessions use a development environment when changes require code. [Connecting a site](/storefront/editor/connect-site) explains their setup.

## Choose a task

- [Create and edit pages](/storefront/editor/content/pages)
- [Use reusable sections](/storefront/editor/content/sections)
- [Upload and select assets](/storefront/editor/content/assets)
- [Set SEO](/storefront/editor/advanced/seo) or [redirect old URLs](/storefront/editor/advanced/redirects)
- [Configure variants](/storefront/editor/advanced/segments)
- [Manage a blog](/storefront/editor/content/blog)

## Versions and migration

These guides target the released Blocks **7.x** runtime and Studio Site Editor. Editor migration and runtime migration have separate lifecycles: use the [migration archive](/storefront/legacy/overview) to plan a move from retired Fresh/admin installations. Blocks Next describes a separate framework contract; it does not replace the released 7.x setup in these guides.

The [legacy admin archive](/storefront/legacy/overview) preserves the former `admin.deco.cx` workflows and screenshots.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/routes/workspace/agent-site-editor.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/connect-site
Document: Connect a site

# Connect a site

> Configure the repository, content editing, and preview server for a Studio site project.

A site project needs a repository containing its code and content, plus a running site that serves compatible CMS metadata and preview endpoints. Start with an existing Blocks storefront or complete the setup guide for the framework version you are using.

## Import the repository

1. Open your organization in [Studio](https://studio.decocms.com).
2. Choose **Import repository** and select the GitHub repository for your site. Give the Studio GitHub connection access to that repository if it is missing from the picker.
3. Open the imported project and its settings.
4. Under **CMS**, enable **Content editing**. Under **Preview**, set the **Preview server** to the running site's URL.
5. Enable **Fast Preview** for content editing through that server, then start a new chat. Existing chats keep the mode they were created with.

The **Import from deco.cx** migration flow is restricted to deco staff in the reviewed implementation. For normal setup, use repository import; coordinate migration of managed site assets with your team.

## Confirm the connection

Open **Content** and verify that the expected pages and sections appear. Open **Preview**, choose a page, and make a small draft edit. Wait for saving to finish and confirm the preview uses the new value before publishing.

A blank catalog usually calls for checking the site metadata, component registration, and server reachability. A CMS session without a repository cannot save through Git; one without a preview server cannot render Fast Preview.

Local preview sessions support local work and do not expose the publishing controls. Use a repository-backed project to [publish or request review](/storefront/editor/content/releases).

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/views/virtual-mcp/index.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/digital-experience
Document: Storefront and Studio

# Storefront and Studio

> Understand the relationship between the storefront offering and the extensible Studio platform.

**Storefront** groups the tools for building and operating websites and apps: the Blocks framework, Site Editor, commerce integrations, analytics, and observability. Its documentation follows concrete work on that digital experience.

**Studio** is the extensible platform those tools use. It supplies shared capabilities for connecting context, organizing tasks, running work in sandboxes, and extending interfaces through MCP Apps. Studio can support applications beyond storefronts.

## Start with your task

If you want to compose a page, configure a product component, preview a campaign, or publish content, start with the [Site Editor](/storefront/editor/overview).

If you want to implement the components and functions that power that site, choose your Blocks framework version. The new `next` framework contract is a preview; existing Fresh and TanStack documentation remains versioned.

If you want to build a new Studio application or connect another system to its work loop, use the Studio developer guides.

## Document the capability where people use it

The Site Editor's user guides stay in Storefront even though the editor runs inside Studio. The generic interface-extension API belongs to Studio. This gives each product one clear entry point while allowing guides to link to their shared platform dependencies.

Product-specific availability depends on the installed integrations and deployment. An extensible platform does not automatically provision every storefront service.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/routes/workspace/agent-site-editor.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/agentic-cms
Document: Content editing with agents

# Content editing with agents

> Combine visual content editing and agent-assisted development in the storefront project.

The Site Editor combines visual content forms with Studio's work tools. Your team can configure content directly or ask an agent to help develop components, adjust content, and investigate a storefront problem.

## Choose the right session

Use a content-editing session with Fast Preview when the change fits the site's existing components and schemas. For a new component, a runtime fix, or another code change, use a coding session with a development environment.

The current Site Editor exposes Preview, Content, and Code in the same project context. Available capabilities depend on the project and session. Fast Preview can support content work without provisioning a full coding sandbox.

## Review the outcome

An agent's change uses the repository workflow. Preview the affected pages and inspect the changed content or code. [Publishing](/storefront/editor/content/releases) follows the project's policy: some changes can publish directly; others need a request for review and repository approval.

A direct publish goes through the underlying GitHub publish sequence. Separate human approval depends on project policy and repository requirements.

## Product boundaries

This guide belongs to **Storefront** because it explains operating a site. Studio's generic documentation explains how to build MCP Apps, connect context, organize tasks, and execute work in sandboxes.

Connecting an existing site to Studio is independent of upgrading its framework. See [Legacy migration](/storefront/legacy/overview) before combining the two changes.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/routes/workspace/agent-site-editor.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/content/pages
Document: Create and edit pages

# Create and edit pages

> Compose a page from registered sections and preview real URLs before publishing.

Pages combine a route with an ordered list of sections. The **name** identifies the page in the editor; the **path** determines its public URL. SEO is configured [separately](/storefront/editor/advanced/seo).

## Create a page

1. Open the site's **Content** view and select **Pages**.
2. Choose the create-page action.
3. Enter a descriptive name and a path such as `/summer-sale`.
4. Choose an existing page as a template if you want to copy its content; otherwise start blank.
5. Create the page, then add sections from the site's component catalog.

The path must start with `/` and cannot contain `..`, a backslash, or a leading `//`. A template copies content; the new page takes the name and path you entered.

## Edit and preview

Select a section to edit its fields. Add, duplicate, remove, or reorder sections to change the composition. Fields are generated from the components registered by your development team.

For dynamic paths such as `/products/:slug` or `/categories/*`, enter a real parameter value in the preview controls. A literal `:slug` is a template, not a product URL. Check the result with representative products or categories.

Changes save to the working branch. Review the desktop and mobile layout and follow [Publish changes](/storefront/editor/content/releases) to promote the draft.

## Avoid unexpected shared changes

A copied page can still reference saved sections shared with other pages. Before editing a header, footer, or other reusable section, check whether you want a global change or a local copy. [Reusable sections](/storefront/editor/content/sections) explains the difference.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sections-editor/create-page-modal.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/content/sections
Document: Edit and reuse sections

# Edit and reuse sections

> Configure components, share saved sections, and detach local copies when needed.

A section is a component your development team makes available to the editor. Its schema determines the text, images, links, lists, and data inputs you can configure.

## Configure a section

1. Open a page in **Preview** or **Content → Pages**.
2. Add a component from the catalog or select an existing section.
3. Fill its required fields and inspect the preview.
4. Wait for autosave before switching contexts or publishing.

Use the section's actions to duplicate or remove it. Reorder the list to change page composition. Removing a section from a page is different from deleting a saved block used elsewhere.

## Make content reusable

Use **Make reusable** to save a section with a meaningful block name. The page then references the saved block. Find saved sections under **Content → Advanced → Sections** and reuse them on other pages.

For example, saving a campaign banner as `summer-banner` lets several pages share the same configuration. Editing that saved banner changes every page referencing it after publication.

If only one page should differ, use **Detach** on that reference to create local content before editing. Duplicating a page does not automatically make all its shared references independent.

## When a field is missing

The editor can configure the fields supplied by the site. Ask a developer to update the component's typed props and generated schema when a new field is needed. Adding a component in code and configuring its saved content are separate operations.

Finish with [Publish changes](/storefront/editor/content/releases); inspect affected pages when you change shared sections.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sections-editor/section-list.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/content/releases
Document: Publish changes

# Publish changes

> Review a draft, publish under the project policy, or submit it for approval.

Editing saves content to the working branch. **Publish** promotes that work through the project's GitHub workflow; **Request review** submits it for approval. Neither saving a field nor seeing it in Preview makes it public.

## Publish a draft

1. Wait for pending saves to finish and preview the pages affected by your change.
2. Open **Publish** in the Site Editor header.
3. Review the changed items and add a note describing the change. Discard an unwanted item from this surface before continuing if needed.
4. Follow the action allowed by the project: publish directly or request approval.
5. After completion, verify the live site. Studio starts a fresh editable draft after publication.

The current publish sequence pushes the branch, synchronizes it with the base branch, opens or updates a pull request, and squash-merges it. Your deployment integration then applies that merged change. Deployment timing depends on the site's infrastructure.

## Review and conflicts

Project publishing policy and repository checks can require approval. **Request review** opens or updates the PR and leaves it unmerged. A human review step is not mandatory for every edit: use the policy configured for the project.

If another change moves the branch after the change list was read, reopen and review the updated list. If synchronization encounters a conflict, resolve it before publishing. If merging fails after the PR opens, the work remains available in that PR.

## Undoing published work

For an unpublished draft, discard the unwanted change. For published work, revert the relevant change in the repository and deploy the resulting version according to your site's workflow. The former admin's **Releases → Revert** screen is documented only in the [legacy archive](/storefront/legacy/admin/content/releases).

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/thread/github/publish-flow.ts). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/content/assets
Document: Upload and select assets

# Upload and select assets

> Use the site asset browser and image fields with configured storage.

The **Assets** view lists files in the storage configuration associated with the site. It appears when the project has a matching site bucket. Image fields can also use the file picker or an existing public URL.

## Upload and use an image

1. Open **Assets** in the site project, or open an image field in a section.
2. Upload a file with the upload control or drag it into the browser or image field.
3. Wait for the upload to complete. In the browser, search for the uploaded file and copy its URL; in the image field, select it from the picker.
4. Check the image in the page preview and publish the content change.

The image field accepts PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, SVG, and AVIF. Dropping several images directly into one image field uses the first accepted image; use the asset browser to upload several files.

## Storage and publication are separate

An upload writes to the configured bucket. Publishing the page saves the reference to that asset in the site's content. Deleting an asset acts on storage directly and can break references in already published pages; publishing a new page version does not restore a deleted object.

## If Assets is missing

Check that the project has a site slug and that your organization has the matching file storage configuration. Repository import alone does not guarantee that existing managed assets are associated with the project. A developer or administrator can configure storage; see [Studio buckets](/studio/data/buckets).

If an image field has no unambiguous upload destination, the picker asks you to choose a configured bucket.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/layouts/main-panel-tabs/assets-tab.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/content/blog
Document: Edit and schedule blog posts

# Edit and schedule blog posts

> Manage posts with the blog app installed by your storefront.

The Site Editor exposes blog collections when it detects a compatible blog app in the site. **Content** then includes posts, authors, categories, context, and scheduling controls appropriate to the app version.

## Edit a post

1. Open **Content → Posts** and create or select a post.
2. Set its title, slug, excerpt, cover image, and the content sections it uses.
3. Select an author and category when appropriate. Configure the blog app's page path so the preview can resolve the post URL.
4. Review the post and its SEO, then [publish the content changes](/storefront/editor/content/releases).

Blog context supplies the brand voice, formats, and recurring topics used by generation. Generated text remains editable and should be reviewed before publication.

## Post status and publishing

The post's publication status controls whether the blog runtime serves it. Publishing the repository change is a separate step that delivers the configuration to the running site.

For the released 7.x workflow, `@decocms/apps-blog` **7.53.0 or later** provides publication status and scheduling. Studio can also detect scheduling support from the running site's schema. Compatibility information for retired Fresh installations belongs to the [migration archive](/storefront/legacy/overview).

For a scheduled post, configure its go-live date and publish those changes ahead of time. The compatible blog app decides when to serve it. An old or unreadable app version can leave scheduling unavailable; update the site's dependency through its normal development workflow before relying on it.

If no blog app is installed, a calendar or status control cannot make a post live.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sandbox/content/blog/blog-capabilities.ts). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/advanced/loaders
Document: Configure and run loaders

# Configure and run loaders

> Inspect available data functions and save reusable input configurations.

A loader retrieves data for the storefront. Its input schema appears as a form in **Content → Advanced → Loaders**; the implementation stays in the site's code.

## Test a loader configuration

1. Open **Loaders** and select a function from the available catalog or a saved loader block.
2. Fill the input fields. If the site exposes a freeform input instead of a detailed schema, use the JSON editor supplied by this surface.
3. Use **Run** when available to invoke it against the configured preview and inspect the returned result.
4. For an available loader, save it under a block name if you want a reusable configuration. Changes to a saved loader autosave.

Running a loader and saving its configuration are separate operations. A successful result confirms that particular invocation; check how the consuming section renders it and then [publish](/storefront/editor/content/releases) the saved changes.

## Reuse data inputs

Select the saved loader in a section field that accepts its result type. Changing a shared saved loader can affect several sections, so inspect representative pages before publishing.

If no form or result is available, check the schema, runtime registration, preview server, and access rules. The editor does not override loader visibility. When adding a new loader, follow the framework version's development API; Fresh signatures and imports are preserved in the [Fresh reference](/storefront/blocks/fresh/concepts/loader).

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sandbox/content/runnable-block-editor.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/advanced/actions
Document: Configure and run actions

# Configure and run actions

> Configure registered actions and understand the effect of invoking them.

An action performs an operation through the storefront runtime. It may write data or call an external service. **Content → Advanced → Actions** shows available actions and saved configurations supplied by the site.

## Configure an action

1. Open **Actions** and select an available action or a saved block.
2. Set its schema fields, or use the JSON editor when the site's inputs are freeform.
3. Save an available action with a block name to reuse its inputs. A saved block autosaves field changes.
4. Connect that configuration to the component or workflow that will call it, then [publish](/storefront/editor/content/releases) the saved changes.

Saving a block records configuration; it does not execute the operation. Conversely, **Run** invokes the action against the preview target immediately. That invocation can have real effects on the service behind the preview, even before the site's content is published. Use inputs and an environment appropriate for the operation.

## Inspect a result

When Run is available, inspect the structured response. Verify the intended external result rather than assuming that a successful HTTP response means the business operation completed.

Available inputs and permissions come from the registered action and runtime. If the action is missing or invocation is denied, ask a developer to check registration, schema, credentials, and access rules. The editor does not bypass authorization or make a private action public.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sandbox/content/runnable-block-editor.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/advanced/apps
Document: Configure storefront apps

# Configure storefront apps

> Edit configuration blocks for installed integrations through the Site Editor.

Storefront apps package components, loaders, actions, and configuration for a site. They differ from **Studio MCP Apps**, which extend the platform's interface. An integration such as a commerce provider belongs to the storefront app catalog.

## Edit installed configuration

1. Open **Content → Advanced → Apps**.
2. Select an existing app configuration block.
3. Edit the fields generated from that app's schema. Use **Content → Site** for the site's main configuration and **SEO** for SEO defaults.
4. Wait for saving, preview the affected pages, and [publish](/storefront/editor/content/releases).

The app form edits saved values. It does not by itself install a dependency, register runtime code, or provision an external service.

## Add an integration

A developer first installs and registers the app using the appropriate framework version and its commerce or integration guide. The site must expose the app metadata and schema before Studio can show its configuration.

Keep credentials in the secret mechanism supported by that integration; do not put an API secret into an ordinary text field because it happens to be available. The field schema and runtime determine how the value is consumed.

If an app has no editable fields or is missing, confirm that it is installed, registered, and available in the site's metadata. Configuring the existing storefront and developing a new Studio MCP App are different tasks.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sandbox/content/app-editor.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/advanced/segments
Document: Configure page and section variants

# Configure page and section variants

> Use matcher-driven content variants and preview the selected experience.

A variant supplies a different page composition or section configuration when a matcher applies. Matchers are supplied by the storefront, so the available rules depend on its registered schemas and runtime.

## Create a variant

1. Open the page or section in the editor and use its add-variant control.
2. Give the variant a clear name, such as `Summer campaign`.
3. Choose a matcher and configure its inputs. For a date-based campaign, use the date/time matcher exposed by your site.
4. Edit the content for that variant and select it in the preview.
5. Check both the variant and default content before [publishing](/storefront/editor/content/releases).

Page variants have their own section compositions; section variants change one part of a page. The default provides the content to use when no configured condition matches.

## Preview and runtime behavior

Selecting a variant forces that experience in the editor preview. It lets you inspect its content even when your current browser would not satisfy the rule. It does not establish which experience a real visitor will receive.

Test the deployed site with requests matching and not matching the rule. When rules overlap, verify their order and the matcher behavior for your framework version. Previewing a campaign is separate from collecting experiment assignments or conversion metrics.

The old admin's experiment dashboard is archived [here](/storefront/legacy/admin/advanced/experiments). For a controlled A/B test, follow the framework's experimentation and analytics contract rather than treating an ordinary variant as a complete experiment.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sections-editor/page-variant-tabs.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/advanced/redirects
Document: Create URL redirects

# Create URL redirects

> Redirect old paths through the site redirect loader and check query parameter behavior.

A redirect maps an old URL to a new destination. In Studio, redirects are content blocks listed under **Content → Redirects**. The running site must include the corresponding redirect loader for these blocks to take effect.

## Add a redirect

1. Open **Content → Redirects** and create a redirect.
2. Set **From** to the source path, for example `/old-collection`.
3. Set **To** to the destination, for example `/collections/summer`.
4. Choose **Temporary** or **Permanent**.
5. Decide whether to discard query parameters, then wait for autosave and [publish](/storefront/editor/content/releases).

Use a temporary redirect while the destination may change. Permanent redirects can be cached by browsers; verify the destination before using them. The reviewed editor labels temporary redirects as 307, while the v7 Worker runtime emits 302 for its temporary content redirects. Check the actual HTTP response for your runtime; see [v7 redirect behavior](/storefront/blocks/v7/content#redirects-in-content).

## Verify the result

Request the old path on the deployed site and inspect the status and `Location` header. Also test a URL with query parameters, such as `/old-collection?utm_source=newsletter`, to confirm the behavior you selected.

```sh
curl -I https://example.com/old-collection
```

If saving a block has no effect after deployment, ask a developer to verify that the runtime discovers the saved redirect blocks. In the existing compatible app contract, the singular redirect block is consumed by the site's plural redirects loader.

A URL redirect does not configure a domain or certificate. Domain and apex redirect setup belongs to the hosting provider; the former managed-admin domain instructions are preserved in the [legacy archive](/storefront/legacy/admin/management/custom-domains).

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sandbox/content/redirect-data.ts). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/editor/advanced/seo
Document: Configure SEO

# Configure SEO

> Set site defaults and page overrides using the storefront SEO schema.

SEO configuration belongs to the storefront and is edited through Studio's content forms. The site supplies the available SEO types and fields.

## Set site defaults

Open **Content → SEO** and configure the site-level values exposed by your SEO block, such as title, description, canonical information, and sharing images. These defaults give ordinary pages a starting point.

## Customize a page

1. Open a page and its SEO editing controls.
2. Select the SEO type appropriate to the page. Commerce pages may expose product or listing schemas instead of the general form.
3. For a general page, leave **Use default** enabled for values inherited from site SEO. Disable it only for fields that need a page-specific value.
4. Save, preview, and [publish](/storefront/editor/content/releases) the change.

Inheritance is per field. Changing a site default can affect every page still inheriting that field; an explicit page override stays independent.

## Check the published output

Inspect the rendered title and metadata on the public page after deployment. For dynamic product or listing routes, check real URLs with representative data. A form preview helps author configuration; the storefront runtime produces the final metadata.

If a desired field is absent, verify the SEO component and schema supplied by the site. A developer can extend those fields or choose a suitable SEO type; the editor cannot invent runtime behavior for an unsupported property.

Implementation reference: [Studio Site Editor](https://github.com/decocms/studio/blob/f4cd364c0be8d4376fde958bf6b2399cf5d5e3e0/apps/web/src/components/sections-editor/general-seo-form.tsx). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/templates/overview
Document: Website templates

# Website templates

> Start with a working website implementation, then adapt its sections, content, and commerce integration.

A template gives you a website repository to start from: routes, UI components, sections, content, and integration code. Copy or fork it, then adapt the implementation to your design and business.

Templates belong to the **Storefront** offering alongside the framework and the editing app. Each has a different role:

| Part | What it provides | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| **Blocks framework** | Typed configuration, content resolution, and the runtime contract your website implements | [Blocks](/storefront/blocks/v7/overview) |
| **Site Editor app** | The Studio interface for editing, previewing, and publishing a connected site's content | [Site Editor](/storefront/editor/overview) |
| **Website template** | A starting implementation of the website itself | [Storefront TanStack](/storefront/templates/storefront-tanstack) |

A template uses the framework; its website can connect to the Site Editor. You can also add Blocks to a website you already own without starting from a template.

## Available template

### Storefront TanStack

[Storefront TanStack](/storefront/templates/storefront-tanstack) is a commerce starter built with TanStack Start, React, and Cloudflare Workers. It includes CMS sections, storefront components, and a Shopify integration. The public source is [deco-sites/storefront-tanstack](https://github.com/deco-sites/storefront-tanstack).

Choose it when you want to begin with an existing storefront implementation and customize the code. Its guide covers the repository layout, local setup, and the site settings you need to make your own.

## Pick the path that fits your project

- **Start a storefront from an implementation:** use [Storefront TanStack](/storefront/templates/storefront-tanstack).
- **Add Blocks to your own application:** follow the [current framework quickstart](/storefront/blocks/v7/quickstart).
- **Connect an existing compatible site for editing:** follow [Connect a site](/storefront/editor/connect-site).
- **Explore the proposed next framework design:** read [How it works](/storefront/blocks/next/how-it-works). The preview has its own API and is separate from the template's current 7.x runtime.

Templates follow their own dependency versions. Check a starter's `package.json` and setup guide before applying framework examples from another version.

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/templates/storefront-tanstack
Document: Storefront TanStack

# Storefront TanStack

> Use the public TanStack Start storefront implementation as the starting point for your website.

[**deco-sites/storefront-tanstack**](https://github.com/deco-sites/storefront-tanstack) is a website implementation built on TanStack Start, React 19, and Cloudflare Workers. Use its sections, storefront components, and commerce wiring as the starting point for a site you own.

The repository configures **Shopify** in its site setup. It includes product and search interfaces, cart state, and editable page content. Having another commerce package installed does not configure that platform for your site; the registered app and domain adapters determine the behavior.

## Framework version

The reviewed [package manifest](https://github.com/deco-sites/storefront-tanstack/blob/7d37b7bd732fdb1da253e3b3f3c3806610d9212e/package.json) declares `^7.20.7` for `@decocms/blocks`, `@decocms/blocks-admin`, `@decocms/blocks-cli`, and `@decocms/tanstack`, with separate commerce app packages. This is the **7.x framework**; use the [current Blocks documentation](/storefront/blocks/v7/overview) alongside this starter.

Some older README sections refer to `@decocms/start` and `@decocms/apps`. The manifest and setup source use the split 7.x packages. Keep the repository's dependency and setup files together when adapting it. The [Next preview](/storefront/blocks/next/how-it-works) describes a separate proposed API.

## Run locally

Use **Node.js 24 or later** and npm, following the [current 7.x runtime requirements](/storefront/blocks/v7/overview#requirements). To inspect the starter locally:

```bash
git clone https://github.com/deco-sites/storefront-tanstack.git my-store
cd my-store
npm install
npm run dev
```

Open the URL printed by Vite, normally `http://localhost:5173`. For a site you intend to connect to Studio, fork the repository into your GitHub organization and clone that fork instead, so the project can save changes to your repository.

## Find the code and content

| Location | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/sections/` | CMS sections such as headers, product details, shelves, and content blocks |
| `src/components/` | Reusable storefront UI |
| `src/routes/` | TanStack routes, including the home and catch-all CMS routes |
| `src/platform/` | Domain state, server actions, hooks, and commerce adapters |
| `.deco/blocks/` | Saved page content and app configuration |
| `src/setup.ts` | Framework setup, section registration, and configured commerce apps |
| `src/worker-entry.ts` | Cloudflare entry with the CMS admin protocol and request handling |
| `wrangler.jsonc` | Worker name, account, bindings, and deployment settings |

The [site setup](https://github.com/deco-sites/storefront-tanstack/blob/7d37b7bd732fdb1da253e3b3f3c3806610d9212e/src/setup.ts) registers the Shopify app and imports generated content and section metadata. The [CMS routes](https://github.com/deco-sites/storefront-tanstack/blob/7d37b7bd732fdb1da253e3b3f3c3806610d9212e/src/routes/%24.tsx) resolve page content and render its sections. Generated `.gen.` files are build outputs; change the source sections or saved content and regenerate them.

## Make it your site

1. Adapt the sections, styles, branding, and page content to your storefront.
2. Configure your Shopify store through the app configuration in `.deco/blocks/deco-shopify.json` and the corresponding site setup. The [Shopify guide](/storefront/commerce/shopify) covers the integration.
3. Set your site identity and production origins in the site and Vite setup. Update `DECO_SITE_NAME`, the Worker name, and the Cloudflare account in `wrangler.jsonc`.
4. Configure the KV bindings, tail-worker service, and telemetry destinations for your own deployment. The repository contains settings for its existing site; use resources belonging to your deployment.
5. [Connect the repository and preview server to Studio](/storefront/editor/connect-site) when you are ready for visual editing. Follow the [Site Editor publishing workflow](/storefront/editor/content/releases) for reviewed content changes.

## Build and deploy

The manifest's build script generates content, sections, loaders, schema, and routes before Vite builds the app:

```bash
npm run typecheck
npm run build
```

Once your Cloudflare account and resources are configured, `npm run deploy` runs the build and Wrangler deployment. See [Hosting](/storefront/hosting/overview) for the deployment workflow and [TanStack integration](/storefront/blocks/v7/tanstack) for the framework's Worker contract.

Source reviewed on 2026-10-05 at commit `7d37b7b`. For moving an existing Fresh site, start with [Migration](/storefront/migration/overview) rather than replacing its code with a template.
