# Migration playbook

> Phase-by-phase walkthrough of moving a Fresh storefront to TanStack Start.

This is the manual walkthrough. If you'd rather automate, run [`deco-migrate`](/storefront/migration/migrate-script) first — it does the mechanical 70%. Read this when the script flags something it won't auto-fix, or when you want to understand what the script is doing.

## Phase 1 — Analyze

Before changing anything, take inventory:

```bash
cd /path/to/v1-site

# section count
find src/sections -name "*.tsx" | wc -l

# island count (must be eliminated)
find src/islands -name "*.tsx" 2>/dev/null | wc -l

# custom routes
find routes -type f | wc -l

# loaders / actions
find src/loaders src/actions -name "*.ts" 2>/dev/null | wc -l

# blocks
find .deco/blocks -name "*.json" | wc -l
```

Write these numbers down. They become the post-migration verification baseline.

Look for:
- Imports of `@deco/deco/*` and `$fresh/*` — the script handles these.
- Imports of `@preact/signals` and `preact/*` — the script rewrites these to React.
- Custom `useScript(fn)` calls — these need manual review.
- Custom `Deno.env` reads — these need conversion.
- Anything in `src/islands/` — every island needs a destination.

## Phase 2 — Scaffold

Generate the v2 baseline files:

- `vite.config.ts` (with `decoVitePlugin`, Cloudflare plugin, TanStack Start plugin, React, Tailwind v4)
- `wrangler.jsonc` (with `nodejs_compat` + `no_handle_cross_request_promise_resolution`)
- `src/setup.ts` (calls `createSiteSetup`)
- `src/server.ts` (TanStack Start server entry)
- `src/worker-entry.ts` (calls `createDecoWorkerEntry`)
- `src/routes/__root.tsx`, `src/routes/index.tsx`, `src/routes/$.tsx`

The script generates these. If you're doing this by hand, copy from the templates in [`decocms/blocks/.agents/skills/deco-to-tanstack-migration/templates/`](https://github.com/decocms/blocks/tree/main/.agents/skills).

## Phase 3 — Transform

This is the bulk-rewrite phase:

- **Imports** — `preact/*` → `react`, `@preact/signals` → `@tanstack/store`, `$fresh/*` → `@tanstack/react-start`, `@deco/deco/*` → `@decocms/blocks/*` and `@decocms/apps-*`.
- **JSX attributes** — `class` → `className`, `for` → `htmlFor`.
- **Fresh APIs** — `defineRoute` / `defineApp` to TanStack Router patterns.
- **Deno-isms** — `Deno.env.get(X)` to env bindings, `import.meta.url` to Vite-friendly equivalents.
- **Tailwind** — v3 → v4 (token-based color references, opacity syntax, `@apply` rewrites).

The 70+ rewrite rules live in the migrate script. See [Migrate script reference](/storefront/migration/migrate-script) for the breakdown.

## Phase 4 — Cleanup

Remove what's no longer needed:

- `src/islands/` — every island became something else.
- `routes/` (the old Fresh routes folder).
- `deno.json`, `deno.lock`.
- `static/` — moved to `public/`.
- Any `compat/` folders your team added during incremental migration attempts.

## Phase 5 — Bootstrap

Install dependencies and regenerate everything:

```bash
npm install
npm run generate:blocks
npm run generate:schema
npm run generate:sections
npm run generate:loaders
npx tsr generate
```

Wire all of these as composite scripts in `package.json` so a single `npm run generate` (or equivalent) keeps everything in sync.

## Phase 6 — Verify

Run the typecheck and build:

```bash
npm run typecheck   # tsc --noEmit
npx vite build      # full prod build
npx wrangler deploy --dry-run
```

A clean typecheck + clean build means the mechanical migration is done. Runtime correctness is a separate pass.

## Phase 7 — Manual fixes

The migrate script logs everything that needs human attention to `MIGRATION_REPORT.md`. The most common items:

### Islands → `"use client"` or hoisted state

Pattern: an island that owned local state (cart drawer toggle, search modal). In React, mark the parent section `"use client"` if the whole section is interactive, or hoist state into a section-level provider.

```tsx
// before (Fresh)
// src/islands/CartDrawer.tsx ← whole island
// src/sections/Header.tsx imports the island

// after (React)
// src/sections/Header/Header.tsx
"use client";



export default function Header({ ... }: Props) {
  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
  return (
    <>
      <header>...</header>
      <CartDrawer open={open} onClose={() => setOpen(false)} />
    </>
  );
}
```

Walk islands one at a time, deciding per island whether it can hoist into the section (server) or needs to remain a client component.

### `useScript(fn)` removal

`useScript(fn)` was a Fresh pattern that extracted a function to an inline `<script>` tag. In v2 it doesn't hydrate cleanly. Replace with:

- **`inlineScript`** from `@decocms/blocks/sdk/useScript` for static script content.
- **A real client component** for anything that touches state or DOM.

### Platform hooks (`useCart`, `useUser`, `useWishlist`)

If your v1 site had custom overrides, port them. The default implementations in `@decocms/apps-vtex/hooks` cover the common cases. See [VTEX hooks](/storefront/commerce/vtex/hooks).

### Third-party `<head>` scripts

Scripts that mutate `<head>` (Google Tag Manager, Adobe Launch, Hotjar) often cause hydration mismatches in React. The fix is to inject them in the worker response, after React has rendered, instead of in JSX.

## Phase 8 — Performance pass

After functional correctness:

- **Tune `foldThreshold`** — usually 2-3 sections eager, rest deferred.
- **Audit cache profiles** — the defaults are conservative; loosen for static content, tighten for cart-adjacent pages.
- **Prune `loaders.gen.ts`** — use `--decofile-dir .deco/blocks` so only loaders the CMS actually references appear in the registry. Recommended for new sites; existing sites can adopt incrementally.
- **Verify deferred sections in dev** — if you hit "I/O across requests" errors, set `no_handle_cross_request_promise_resolution` in `wrangler.jsonc`.

## Phase 9 — QA and deploy

Use the [migration checklist](/storefront/migration/checklist) to walk through verification.

## See also

- [Migrate script](/storefront/migration/migrate-script)
- [Agent skills](/storefront/migration/agent-skills)
- [Migration checklist](/storefront/migration/checklist)
- [Worker entry reference](/storefront/blocks/v7/framework/worker-entry)

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/migrate-script
Document: Migrate script

# Migrate script

> What npx -p @decocms/blocks-cli deco-migrate actually does.

`@decocms/blocks-cli` provides `deco-migrate` for migrating a retired Fresh/Deno site to the released Blocks 7.x TanStack binding. This page documents what it does, what flags it accepts, and what it doesn't.

## Quick start

```bash
# from your v1 site directory
npx -p @decocms/blocks-cli deco-migrate
```

That's it. The script analyzes the source layout, scaffolds Blocks 7.x files, transforms imports/JSX/APIs, cleans up dead code, generates a report, verifies the result, and bootstraps deps.

## Flags

| Flag | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `--source <dir>` | Source directory (default: current directory) |
| `--dry-run` | Preview changes without writing files |
| `--verbose` | Show detailed output for every transformation |
| `--no-compile` | Skip the post-transform `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` step |
| `--no-cleanup-audit` | Skip the post-migration audit (read-only sanity check) |
| `--strict` | Fail the run on any cleanup audit warning |
| `--with-build` | Run a full `vite build` (default is typecheck only) |
| `--help`, `-h` | Show help |

## The seven phases

Pulled from `scripts/migrate.ts`:

```
1. Analyze   — scan source, detect Preact/Fresh/Deco patterns
2. Scaffold  — generate vite.config.ts, wrangler.jsonc, routes, setup.ts, worker-entry
3. Transform — rewrite imports (70+ rules), JSX attrs, Fresh APIs, Deno-isms, Tailwind v3→v4
4. Cleanup   — delete islands/, old routes, deno.json, move static/ → public/
5. Report    — generate MIGRATION_REPORT.md with manual review items
6. Verify    — 18+ smoke tests (zero old imports, scaffolded files exist)
7. Bootstrap — Bun install, generate CMS blocks, generate routes
```

After phase 7, the script optionally:

- **Compiles** (`--no-compile` to skip): runs `tsc --noEmit` and (with `--with-build`) `vite build`. Compile failures are reported but don't fail the migration unless `--strict`.
- **Cleanup audit** (`--no-cleanup-audit` to skip): a read-only audit of leftover Fresh patterns. Surfaces things like leftover `compat/` folders or stray `useScript(fn)` calls.

## Layout detection

Phase 0 accepts **classic** Fresh projects with `sections/`, `islands/`, and related directories at the repository root, and **modern** projects with those directories under `src/`. Both layouts are scanned natively.

A **mixed** layout (both populated root and `src/` directories) or an **empty** layout aborts before migration writes begin. Check the source path or restore a clean source checkout rather than rearranging a supported `src/` layout. A dry run uses the same preflight.

## What `MIGRATION_REPORT.md` contains

After a real run, the report has:

- **Run mode** (DRY_RUN or EXECUTED) and timestamp.
- **File counts** — scaffolded, transformed, deleted.
- **Full file lists** for each category.
- **Loader inventory** — every `loaders/*.ts` and `actions/*.ts` found, with port status.
- **Tailwind v3 → v4 pitfalls** — negative `z-index`, opacity migrations, `@apply` rewrites.
- **`@decocms/blocks` boilerplate duplication** — places where the migrated code overlaps with framework-provided helpers (candidates for cleanup).
- **Concrete next steps** — exact commands to run.

The report is written at the site root. Review it and commit it with the migration so reviewers can inspect what changed.

## Configuration file

You can pin script behavior via `.deco-migrate.config.json` at your project root:

```json
{
  "sectionConventions": {
    "extend": {
      "eagerSync": ["Header"],
      "sync": ["Hero"],
      "listingCache": ["ProductShelf"],
      "staticCache": ["Footer"]
    }
  }
}
```

`extend` adds section file names to the defaults; `replace` uses only your lists. This file does not implement `exclude`, `skipPhases`, or `verbose`; use the documented CLI flags and review the migration diff. See [section conventions](/storefront/blocks/v7/migrate-from-fresh#tune-section-conventions).

## Companion scripts

### `deco-post-cleanup`

Run after the migration to clean up leftover boilerplate the framework has since absorbed:

```bash
npx -p @decocms/blocks-cli deco-post-cleanup
```

This is read + write — review the diff before committing.

### `deco-htmx-analyze`

Specifically for sites that used HTMX during the v1 era. Reports HTMX usage so you can plan the conversion to React patterns. HTMX is **not shipped in `@decocms/blocks`** — sites that depended on it need to rewrite to React.

```bash
npx -p @decocms/blocks-cli deco-htmx-analyze
```

## What the script doesn't do

- **Doesn't migrate `useCart` / `useUser` / `useWishlist` overrides.** If your v1 site monkey-patched these, you'll need to port the overrides manually using the v2 hook factories. See [VTEX hooks](/storefront/commerce/vtex/hooks).
- **Doesn't migrate custom worker logic.** Custom proxies, A/B harnesses, edge transforms — the script generates a vanilla `worker-entry.ts` and leaves you to layer custom logic on top using [`createDecoWorkerEntry`](/storefront/blocks/v7/framework/worker-entry).
- **Doesn't fix runtime correctness.** A successful migration produces compiling code; runtime issues (cart not loading, PDP showing wrong product, hydration mismatches) need human attention.
- **Doesn't translate non-storefront sites.** The script is tuned for the deco.cx storefront archetype. Marketing sites with very custom Fresh setups should expect more manual work.

## Troubleshooting

### "Layout not classic — aborting"

The current migrator accepts root and `src/` layouts. For mixed or empty input, verify `--source` and restore a complete source checkout; read the specific CLI diagnostic before changing directories.

### `tsc --noEmit` fails after migration

Read `MIGRATION_REPORT.md`. Common culprits:
- A platform hook (`useCart`, etc.) that needs site-specific implementation.
- A `useScript(fn)` that the script transformed but the calling code doesn't accept.
- A custom matcher or loader that uses Deno-only APIs.

### `vite build` fails

Run with `--with-build` to surface this during migration. Then read the error — most are missing imports caused by an island that was deleted but still referenced.

### Production deploy works but admin preview is broken

You probably moved admin handlers into the TanStack server entry instead of the worker entry. See [Worker entry reference](/storefront/blocks/v7/framework/worker-entry).

## See also

- [Migration overview](/storefront/migration/overview)
- [Migration playbook](/storefront/migration/playbook)
- [Agent skills](/storefront/migration/agent-skills)
- [Migration checklist](/storefront/migration/checklist)

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/migrate-from-v1
Document: Migrate from Fresh

# Migrate from Fresh

> Move a Fresh/Deno deco.cx storefront to TanStack Start.

Fresh and the former deco.cx admin are retired. For an existing Fresh site, migrate the runtime to released Blocks 7.x and connect the Site Editor separately. The migration script automates mechanical transformations. An agent can help review its report and make site-specific changes; your team still validates behavior, integrations, and deployment.

This page is the **decision tree** — the actual playbook lives under [Migration →](/storefront/migration/overview).

## When should I migrate?

| You should migrate **now** if… | You should **wait** if… |
|---|---|
| You're investing in new features and want React + the broader ecosystem | You're in a feature freeze before a major sales event |
| You're hitting Deno Deploy concurrency limits or cold-start issues | You depend on islands or Preact-specific patterns that aren't yet ported |
| You want first-class edge caching on Cloudflare | Your team is mid-redesign and can't absorb a stack change |
| Your team already writes React day-to-day and Preact friction is real | You're happy on v1 and don't have a forcing function |

Fresh documentation is retained as an archive for existing installations, not an ongoing maintenance promise.

## What's automatic vs. manual

**Automated by `deco-migrate`:**
- Import rewrites (Preact → React, `$fresh/*` → TanStack, `@deco/deco/*` → `@decocms/blocks/*`).
- Vite + Wrangler scaffolding.
- Worker entry + setup file generation.
- Tailwind v3 → v4 token fixes.
- Deletion of `islands/`, old Fresh routes, `deno.json`, `static/` (moved to `public/`).

**Manual fixes (the script flags these):**
- Platform hook implementations (custom `useCart`, `useUser`, `useWishlist` overrides).
- `useScript(fn)` patterns that no longer hydrate cleanly — switch to `inlineScript` or move to `"use client"`.
- Third-party `<head>` scripts that cause CLS.
- Any `compat/` shims your team built.
- Site-specific worker logic (custom proxies, AB harnesses).

## The three paths

### Path A — Run the script directly

```bash
# from your v1 site directory
npx -p @decocms/blocks-cli deco-migrate
```

This runs the 7-phase migration in-place and produces a `MIGRATION_REPORT.md` with the manual TODOs. See [Migration script reference](/storefront/migration/migrate-script).

### Path B — Use the Agent Skill

If you use Claude Code, Cursor, or Codex, install the migration skill and let the agent drive:

```bash
npx skills add decocms/blocks
```

Then in your editor: *"migrate this project to TanStack Start."* The skill knows what's automated and what isn't, and walks the manual fixes interactively. See [Agent skills](/storefront/migration/agent-skills).

### Path C — Manual port

For small storefronts (< 30 sections) or for teams who want full control, follow the [Start fresh](/storefront/blocks/v7/setup/start-fresh) recipe to hand-create the minimum project layout, then copy your sections, components, and `.deco/blocks/` content over.

## What "done" looks like

Use the [post-migration checklist](/storefront/migration/checklist) to verify:

- `tsc --noEmit` is clean.
- `vite build` produces a bundle without warnings about missing imports.
- `wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeds.
- The site renders the home page locally with no console errors.
- Registered sections appear in Studio after the site connection is configured; verify `/live/_meta` metadata separately.

## See also

- [Migration overview](/storefront/migration/overview)
- [Migration playbook](/storefront/migration/playbook)
- [Migration script](/storefront/migration/migrate-script)
- [Migration checklist](/storefront/migration/checklist)

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/agent-skills
Document: Agent skills

# Agent skills

> Use Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, or any AI coding tool to drive the migration.

The `decocms/blocks` repository provides an Agent Skill — a structured prompt + reference set — designed for AI coding tools (Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, etc.). Once installed, you can tell the agent *"migrate this project to TanStack Start"* and it handles the long tail of manual fixes.

## Why a skill (not just a prompt)

A bare prompt sends the agent into the codebase blind. A skill gives the agent:

- The full **playbook** with phase ordering.
- **Reference templates** for every generated file (`vite.config.ts`, `wrangler.jsonc`, `setup.ts`, `worker-entry.ts`, root route).
- **Documented gotchas** — 18+ documented edge cases the script alone can't handle.
- **Decision rules** for tradeoffs the script doesn't take a position on (which sections to defer, which islands need `"use client"` vs hoisting, etc.).

The skill knows what's automated, what's manual, and where to read for context.

## Installation

```bash
npx skills add decocms/blocks --skill deco-to-tanstack-migration
```

This installs the skill into your editor's local skill registry. Supported editors:

- **Claude Code** (`~/.claude/skills/`)
- **Cursor** (`~/.cursor/skills/`)
- **Codex** (`~/.codex/skills/`)
- Any tool that reads the Agent Skill convention

You can also invoke the skill from any of these tools without prior installation if they support remote skill loading.

## Usage

Once the skill is installed, open your v1 storefront in the editor and prompt:

> migrate this project to TanStack Start

The agent will:

1. Run `deco-migrate --dry-run` first and read the report.
2. Confirm the source uses a supported root or src/ layout; resolve mixed or empty input before migrating.
3. Execute the migration script.
4. Walk through every entry in `MIGRATION_REPORT.md` and either fix it or ask you to.
5. Run `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` and resolve errors interactively.
6. Show you a summary diff before committing.

## What the skill is good at

- **Mechanical fixes** — anything regex-able (imports, JSX attrs, Tailwind tokens).
- **Pattern recognition** — spotting `useScript(fn)` calls, identifying islands that should hoist vs. become client components.
- **Cross-referencing** — checking your code against the framework's canonical patterns and documented APIs.
- **Documentation lookup** — when it doesn't know something, it reads `@decocms/blocks`'s docs first instead of guessing.

## What the skill is not good at

- **Business logic decisions.** If your v1 site has a custom checkout flow, the skill can port the syntax but you need to verify the semantics.
- **Performance tuning.** Choosing the right `foldThreshold` or cache profile is product-judgement work; the skill doesn't have your traffic data.
- **Runtime debugging beyond compile-clean.** Cart not adding items? PDP showing the wrong variant? You'll need to debug yourself; the skill can help you instrument but can't see your VTEX account.

## What's in the skill

The skill bundle (lives at [`decocms/blocks/.agents/skills/deco-to-tanstack-migration/`](https://github.com/decocms/blocks/tree/main/.agents/skills/deco-to-tanstack-migration)) contains:

- **`SKILL.md`** — the entry point the agent reads first.
- **`templates/`** — reference files the agent copies and customizes:
  - `vite-config.md`
  - `wrangler-config.md`
  - `setup-ts.md`
  - `worker-entry.md`
  - `package-json.md`
  - `router.md`
  - `root-route.md`
- **`references/`** — deep-dives on specific gotchas:
  - `gotchas.md`
  - `hydration.md`
  - `async-rendering.md`
  - `commerce.md`
  - `htmx-migration.md`
  - `migrations.md`

When the agent encounters something out of the ordinary, it looks up the relevant reference doc instead of hallucinating.

## A second skill: `deco-migrate-script`

There's also a dedicated skill for working on the migration script itself:

```bash
npx skills add decocms/blocks --skill deco-migrate-script
```

This is for **framework contributors** who want to extend the migration script — adding new transform rules, new scaffold templates, etc. End users don't need it.

## Tips for getting the most out of the skill

- **Commit your v1 work first.** The skill makes large, sweeping changes. Have a clean baseline to revert to.
- **Run on a branch.** Don't migrate `main`. Push the branch and review the diff.
- **Don't pre-fix things.** Run the skill on the unmodified v1 codebase. If you've already half-migrated, the skill will be confused by the partial state.
- **Ask follow-up questions.** When the agent makes a decision, ask why. The skill includes the reasoning.
- **Validate with the checklist.** After the skill says "done", run through [the migration checklist](/storefront/migration/checklist) yourself.

## Skill drift

<DocCallout type="info">
Skills are maintained in the framework repository independently of your installed npm package. Review skill updates for your migration target, then install the named skill again when needed. The [skills CLI](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills) supports selecting a skill with `--skill`.
</DocCallout>

- [Migration overview](/storefront/migration/overview)
- [Migration playbook](/storefront/migration/playbook)
- [Migrate script](/storefront/migration/migrate-script)
- [Migration checklist](/storefront/migration/checklist)

# Validate & capture with parity

> Use parity to extract a live storefront and validate the migrated site — see the Parity section.

[`@decocms/parity`](https://github.com/decocms/parity) helps at both ends of a migration:

- **Capture** a live storefront (theme, fonts, components, and — for VTEX IO — the block tree + CMS content) into an agent-ready bundle with [`parity migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate).
- **Validate** the migrated site against the original with [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run).

<DocCallout type="info">
Full documentation lives in the **[Parity section](/storefront/migration/parity/overview)** — every command, the module system, focused runs (pixel-perfect, SEO, cache, vitals), configuration, and CI setup.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [Parity overview](/storefront/migration/parity/overview)
- [`parity migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate) · [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run)

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/overview
Document: Parity overview

# Parity overview

> A CLI that captures a live storefront into an agent-ready bundle and validates a migrated site against the original.

[`@decocms/parity`](https://github.com/decocms/parity) is a Playwright-based CLI with two jobs in a migration:

- **Capture** — point it at a **live storefront URL** and it extracts the theme, fonts, logo/icons, per-component HTML (+ suggested Tailwind + e2e selectors) and, for VTEX IO, the real block tree and CMS content, into a token-lean, **agent-ready bundle**.
- **Validate** — give it two URLs (`prod` = source of truth, `cand` = the migrated site) and it reports UI, functional, SEO, performance, console, network and cache regressions with an LLM-ranked report.

<DocCallout type="info">
Capture works **whether or not you have the source code** — it reads the rendered page. It's the fastest way to start a migration from a store you only have a URL for.
</DocCallout>

## Install

```bash
# Global (auto-installs Playwright Chromium on first run)
npm i -g @decocms/parity

# Or run without installing
npx @decocms/parity run --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev --preset smoke --open
```

Set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) to enable the LLM passes (issue ranking, visual semantic diff, selector discovery). Everything degrades gracefully without a key — see [Configuration](/storefront/migration/parity/config).

## The command families

| Family | Commands | Use it to… |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Validate (prod × cand)** | [`run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run), [`journey`](/storefront/migration/parity/journey), [`pr`](/storefront/migration/parity/ci), `compare` | Compare a migrated site to the original and gate a PR. |
| **Pixel-perfect** | [`fix`, `section`](/storefront/migration/parity/pixel-perfect) | Diff one section (screenshots + heatmap + computed styles) into an LLM-ready bundle. |
| **Performance** | [`vitals`, `cache`](/storefront/migration/parity/performance) | Compare Web Vitals / cache coverage across many pages. |
| **Single-site** | [`e2e`, `audit`](/storefront/migration/parity/single-site) | Validate ONE site end-to-end (no baseline). |
| **Capture** | [`migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate), [`extract`](/storefront/migration/parity/extract) | Extract a store into a migration/agent bundle. |
| **Inspect** | [`css-trace`, `console`, `html`, `check`](/storefront/migration/parity/commands) | Sub-10s single-purpose probes. |
| **Manage** | [`serve`, `report`, `baseline`, `list`, `prompt`, `explain`, `learned`](/storefront/migration/parity/commands) | Work with saved runs + artifacts. |

## Which command?

- **Two URLs, "did anything regress?"** → [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run) (`--preset smoke` first, then `--preset full`).
- **One section looks off** → [`parity fix`](/storefront/migration/parity/pixel-perfect) (pixel diff + heatmap + prompt).
- **Only care about SEO / vitals / cache / DOM** → `parity run --only seo` (or `vitals`/`cache`/`html`) — see [Focused runs](/storefront/migration/parity/run#focused-runs).
- **One live URL, "does it work?"** → [`parity e2e`](/storefront/migration/parity/single-site).
- **Rebuild a store from a URL** → [`parity migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate).
- **In CI** → [`parity pr` / `parity journey`](/storefront/migration/parity/ci).

## See also

- [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run) — the flagship validator
- [`parity migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate) — capture a store
- [Configuration](/storefront/migration/parity/config) · [Checks reference](/storefront/migration/parity/checks)
- [`@decocms/parity` on GitHub](https://github.com/decocms/parity)

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/journey
Document: parity journey

# parity journey

> Run only the purchase journey, step-by-step, prod vs cand — the tightest CI loop.

`parity journey` runs **only the purchase journey** (home → PLP → PDP → shipping → cart → checkout) step-by-step on both sides. It skips the sitemap crawl and the LLM aggregation, so it's the **cheapest CI loop** for "is checkout still working?".

```bash
parity journey --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev \
  --cep 01310-100 --junit results.xml --github
```

| Flag | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `--cep` | CEP for the shipping-calculation step (default `01310-100`) |
| `--junit <file>` | Emit JUnit XML for CI test reporting |
| `--github` | Emit GitHub annotations + write `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` |
| `--json` | One-line JSON verdict to stdout |
| `--no-report` | Skip the HTML report (fastest) |
| `--accept-prod-quirks` | Don't fail on known prod-side quirks (e.g. VTEX seller-null) |

Writes `report.html` / `report.json` plus the optional JUnit XML / GH annotations.

<DocCallout type="info">
Use `journey` in the tight per-commit loop and a full [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run) (or [`parity pr`](/storefront/migration/parity/ci)) for the deep audit.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [CI/CD & workflows](/storefront/migration/parity/ci) — wiring `journey` / `pr` into GitHub Actions
- [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run)

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/commands
Document: Commands reference

# Commands reference

> Every parity command — inspect probes, run management, and the full flag surface.

The full command list. The flagship commands have their own pages ([`run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run), [`migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate), [`fix`/`section`](/storefront/migration/parity/pixel-perfect), [`vitals`/`cache`](/storefront/migration/parity/performance), [`e2e`/`audit`](/storefront/migration/parity/single-site), [`journey`](/storefront/migration/parity/journey), [`extract`](/storefront/migration/parity/extract), [`pr`](/storefront/migration/parity/ci)); the rest are here.

## Inspect — sub-10s single-purpose probes

| Command | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `css-trace --selector <s> [--url \| --prod/--cand]` | List every CSS rule affecting an element, or diff computed styles prod vs cand. `--filter`, `--json`. |
| `console --url <u>` | Capture console errors/warnings + network failures on one page. `--filter error,warning`, `--wait`, `--json`. |
| `html --url <u> [--prod/--cand --diff] --selector <s>` | Dump a page/selector's HTML, or a unified prod × cand diff. `--pretty`, `--json`. |
| `check <name> --prod --cand` | Run ONE named check on a single page (skips sitemap + LLM). See [Checks](/storefront/migration/parity/checks). |

## Manage — saved runs & artifacts

| Command | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list` / `list modules --json` | List saved runs; list the 8 selectable modules. |
| `report <runId> [--section <name>] [--json]` | Reopen a run's HTML report, or extract one tab (HTML / JSON projection). |
| `serve <runId> [--port]` | Local server for a run's report + an iframe proxy that strips `X-Frame-Options`/CSP for the side-by-side view. |
| `compare <runId> --against <baseline>` | Compare a run to a saved baseline. |
| `baseline set <runId> --name` / `list` / `unset <name>` | Manage baselines. |
| `prompt <runId> [--min-severity] [--limit]` | Export a run's ranked issues as a Markdown prompt for any LLM. |
| `explain <runId> <issueId>` | LLM deep-dive on one issue (needs an API key). |
| `learned stats` / `learned validate --url` | Inspect / validate the learned-selectors library. |

<DocCallout type="info">
Run `parity <command> --help` for the exact, current flags — this page summarizes; `--help` is authoritative.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run) · [Checks reference](/storefront/migration/parity/checks) · [Configuration](/storefront/migration/parity/config)

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/config
Document: Configuration

# Configuration

> .parityrc.json selectors + run defaults, .parityignore noise filters, and environment variables.

Two optional files at the project root, both **gitignored by default** (per-user, not per-repo).

## `.parityrc.json`

Selector overrides and run defaults. Any key you don't set falls back to **defaults → learned-selectors → LLM discovery**, in that order — so you rarely need to write selectors by hand.

```json
{
  "cep": "01310-100",
  "selectors": {
    "categoryLink": "header a[href*='/c/']",
    "productCard": "[data-product-card] a",
    "buyButton": "button:has-text('Comprar')",
    "minicartTrigger": "[data-minicart-trigger]",
    "checkoutButton": "a:has-text('Finalizar compra')",
    "searchInput": "input[type='search']",
    "cartCouponInput": "input[name*='coupon']",
    "paginationNext": "a[rel='next']"
  },
  "search": { "terms": ["camisa", "promocao"] },
  "footer": { "maxLinks": 20, "followExternal": false },
  "notFound": { "testUrl": "/this-page-definitely-does-not-exist" },
  "login": { "enabled": true },
  "coupon": { "invalidCode": "INVALIDCOUPON123-XYZ", "validCode": "PARITY10" },
  "serverFnFloodBudget": 10,
  "serverFnPattern": "_serverFn",
  "addToCartConfirmMs": 2000,
  "overlaySelectors": ["#NewsletterPopup", "[data-newsletter-popup]"],
  "minicartPanel": "[data-qa-minicart]"
}
```

`selectors` has ~28 keys (cart / PDP-gallery / login / pagination / search included). `serverFnFloodBudget` / `serverFnPattern` tune the `serverfn-hover-flood` check (default: 10 requests matching `_serverFn`, TanStack Start's convention — override for other frameworks; it skips cleanly when the pattern never matches).

## `.parityignore`

Noise filters — visual regions, network URLs, and console messages to ignore (one pattern per line, `#` for comments). Use it to silence known-flaky third-party widgets, analytics beacons, or intentional design differences.

## Environment variables

| Variable | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | Enables Claude passes (issue ranking, visual semantic diff, selector discovery). |
| `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` | Alternative LLM provider. |
| Login credentials | For the opt-in `login` flow (see `--login-email`/`--login-password`). |
| `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` | Set by GitHub Actions — `--github` writes the PR/run summary here. |

<DocCallout type="info">
Without any LLM key, `parity` still runs — issues are severity-sorted (not LLM-ranked), the visual diff keeps only the pixelmatch heatmap, and selectors fall back to defaults/learned. The CLI never fails just because the LLM is unavailable.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run) · [Checks reference](/storefront/migration/parity/checks)

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/checks
Document: Checks reference

# Checks reference

> Every check parity runs, what it catches, and its module (the --only/--skip scoping key).

The **Module** column is the `--only` / `--skip` scoping key for [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run#focused-runs) (or run `parity list modules`). Results are aggregated (optionally via Claude) and ranked by severity; each issue includes screenshots, reproduction, and a suggested fix.

| Check | What it catches | Module |
| --- | --- | --- |
| HTTP status parity | Routes that 404 / 500 in cand but worked in prod | `seo` |
| Console errors | New hydration mismatches, failed fetches, JS exceptions | `console` |
| HTML structural diff | Section / element counts drifting beyond tolerance | `html` |
| Meta / SEO parity | `<title>`, meta description, canonical, `og:*`, twitter, JSON-LD | `seo` |
| Visual diff (LLM Vision) | Sections missing, wrong hero, broken shelf, layout shifts | `visual` |
| Purchase journey | Home → PLP → PDP → CEP → cart → checkout completes in both | `e2e` |
| PLP pagination | Next-page / load-more / infinite-scroll work; `?page=N` fallback | `seo` |
| Network summary | Request count / bytes / cache hit rate | `network` |
| Web Vitals | LCP, FCP, TTFB, INP, CLS — mobile + desktop | `vitals` |
| Image loading health | Missing alt, no srcset, broken `<img>` | `html` |
| Lazy section presence | Deco render/loader routes responding | `html` |
| Banner aspect ratio | Hero/banner images keep the same ratio (CLS/crop) | `visual` |
| SEO deep audit | robots.txt, sitemap, noindex regressions | `seo` |
| Cache coverage | Cache hit rate, opportunities to cache | `cache` |
| Search presence / autocomplete / results / no-results | Search reachable, suggestions, comparable counts, empty state | `e2e` |
| Cart interactions | Multi-item add / qty / persistence / coupon / remove | `e2e` |
| 404 parity | Invalid URL returns 404 (no catch-all 200) | `seo` |
| Cookie/CEP modal CLS | Modals don't shift layout > 0.1 | `visual` |
| PDP gallery + related | Gallery + "Related products" shelf render | `e2e` |
| PDP breadcrumbs | Breadcrumb (markup or JSON-LD) renders on PDP | `e2e` |
| PLP sorting | `?sort=`/`?orderBy=` actually reorders products | `e2e` |
| Footer links health | Institutional links aren't broken | `seo` |
| Login flow _(opt-in)_ | Valid creds log in; invalid ones show an error | `e2e` |
| Picture missing dims | `<picture>` without width/height (static CLS) | `html` |
| Cart reveal mode | prod/cand cart-reveal markup intent matches | `e2e` |
| SPA navigation | Client-side route change doesn't drop CMS sections vs F5 | `e2e` |
| Server-fn hover flood | Hovering cards doesn't flood `_serverFn`/preload requests | `e2e` |

## Running one check

```bash
parity check meta-seo-parity --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev
```

## See also

- [`parity run` — focused runs](/storefront/migration/parity/run#focused-runs)
- [Configuration](/storefront/migration/parity/config) — selectors + flow config the checks use

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/run
Document: parity run

# parity run

> Compare two URLs (prod × cand) and produce a parity report — with module selection for focused runs (SEO, visual, cache, vitals, DOM/CSS).

`parity run` is the flagship command: it drives **two URLs** — `prod` (the source of truth) and `cand` (the migrated site / PR preview) — through the same flows and checks, then reports what differs.

```bash
# Fast smoke (~30s, no LLM) — validate the URLs respond and render
parity run --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev --preset smoke --open

# Full audit with visual diff (needs an LLM key)
parity run --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev --preset full --open
```

## Presets

| Preset | What it runs |
| --- | --- |
| `smoke` | Homepage, mobile only, no LLM, no visual/vitals crawl. ~30s — cheap enough to ping after every deploy. |
| `full` | Purchase-journey + search + cart-interactions, both viewports, vitals-pages 10 / visual-pages 5. Deep audit. |
| `ci` | Purchase-journey, mobile, vitals-pages 5 / visual-pages 3, `--only e2e,html,console`. Tuned for CI. |

Your explicit flags always win over the preset.

## Output

A run writes `parity-output/runs/<runId>/report.json` (the machine-readable source of truth) and `report.html`. Key `report.json` fields:

- `verdict` — `status` (pass/warn/fail), `score` (0–100), severity counts, `modulesRun`. The score reflects **only the modules that ran** and rises as issues are fixed.
- `topIssues` — LLM-ranked, de-duplicated (5–10 items); each has a `severity`, `check`, and `suggestedFix`.
- `moduleVerdicts[]` — per-module score/status + `pagesAnalyzed`.
- `visualDiff` — per-page prod/cand screenshots, a **pixelmatch heatmap**, sections missing in cand, and (with an LLM) semantic diffs. `visualDiff.parityOk` is the binary "is it rendering right?" signal.

<DocCallout type="info">
Use `--json runs.jsonl` to stream one line per check as it completes, and `parity report <runId> --section <name> --json` to extract one tab as structured JSON — both are designed for agents driving `parity` in a loop.
</DocCallout>

## Focused runs

Every check belongs to exactly one **module**. Select what runs with `--only` / `--skip`; the **score reflects only what ran**. `--why` prints the selection reasoning.

| Module | Focus | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `e2e` | Purchase journey, search, cart, login, PDP/PLP | `--only e2e` |
| `seo` | Meta tags, deep SEO audit, 404, pagination, HTTP status | `--only seo` |
| `visual` | **Pixel-perfect** — visual regression + pixelmatch heatmap + LLM Vision diff | `--only visual` |
| `vitals` | Web Vitals (LCP/FCP/TTFB/INP/CLS) | `--only vitals` |
| `cache` | Cache coverage / MISS opportunities | `--only cache` |
| `console` | Console errors + hydration warnings | `--only console` |
| `html` | **Component DOM/CSS** — structural diff, lazy sections, image dims | `--only html` |
| `network` | Request-count / payload deltas | `--only network` |

```bash
# Only SEO
parity run --prod ... --cand ... --only seo

# Everything except visual (skip the LLM Vision cost)
parity run --prod ... --cand ... --skip visual

# One specific check, plus explain the selection
parity run --prod ... --cand ... --only check:meta-seo-parity --why
```

<DocCallout type="info">
For the deepest **pixel-perfect** loop on a single section (screenshots + heatmap + computed styles + an LLM-ready prompt), use [`parity fix` / `parity section`](/storefront/migration/parity/pixel-perfect) instead of a full run.
</DocCallout>

## Picking pages

By default `run` samples a representative set from prod's sitemap. Force exact coverage with `--pages "/,/account,/p/known"` or `--pages-file targets.txt`. These scope the visual-diff / vitals passes; the flows crawl always runs.

## Key flags

| Flag | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `--prod` / `--cand` | required | The two URLs to compare |
| `--preset` | — | `smoke` \| `full` \| `ci` |
| `--only` / `--skip` / `--why` | — | Module/check selection (see above) |
| `--viewports` | `mobile,desktop` | Viewports to test |
| `--no-visual-diff` | visual on | Skip the visual-diff pass |
| `--no-cache` / `--bypass-cache` | cache on | Ignore / wipe the cross-run verdict cache |
| `--fail-on` | `critical` | Exit 1 when an issue at/above this severity is hit |
| `--llm` | auto | `anthropic` \| `openrouter` \| `claude-code` \| `none` \| `auto` |
| `--json` | off | Stream JSONL of check results |

## See also

- [Pixel-perfect (`fix` / `section`)](/storefront/migration/parity/pixel-perfect)
- [Performance (`vitals` / `cache`)](/storefront/migration/parity/performance)
- [CI/CD & workflows](/storefront/migration/parity/ci) · [Checks reference](/storefront/migration/parity/checks)

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/extract
Document: parity extract

# parity extract

> Single-site, AI-ready component extraction — HTML, computed styles, screenshots, assets per component.

`parity extract` is the lower-level, single-site capture: it snapshots one site's UI **components** (header, footer, nav, shelves, hero, minicart, …) into a structured, AI-ready bundle — no prod × cand comparison.

```bash
parity extract --url https://loja.com \
  --pages /,category-auto,pdp-auto --format both
```

Use it when there's no source code to read and you want the raw component inventory to feed an AI agent. ([`parity migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate) builds on the same primitives and adds theme, target playbooks, VTEX content, and a visual report.)

## What it captures

A heuristic pass **always** runs (no LLM required): semantic tags (`header`/`footer`/`nav`), Deco `[data-section]`, class heuristics (`shelf`/`carousel`/`minicart`), and a geometry pass for hero/banner. An optional LLM pass relabels the detected roles.

Per component: rendered HTML, computed styles, a cropped screenshot, and asset/link/text inventories.

## Flags

| Flag | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `--url` | required | Site to extract from |
| `--pages` | `/` | Literal paths and/or `category-auto` / `pdp-auto` |
| `--components` | all | Role allowlist (e.g. `header,footer,shelf`) |
| `--viewport` | `mobile` | `mobile` \| `desktop` \| `tablet` |
| `--format` | `both` | `md` \| `json` \| `both` |
| `--out` | `./parity-extract` | Output directory |
| `--no-llm` | LLM on | Skip the relabel pass |

## Output

`parity-extract/<host>/<timestamp>/` — `index.md` (overview), `manifest.json` (full bundle), and `components/<role>/` (README + `component.html` + `styles.json` + `screenshot.png`).

<DocCallout type="warning">
Keep `parity-extract/` **gitignored** — it contains a real store's content.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [`parity migrate`](/storefront/migration/parity/migrate) — the full migration bundle

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/migrate
Document: parity migrate

# parity migrate

> Phased, target-agnostic capture of a live storefront into an agent-ready migration bundle.

`parity migrate` captures a **live storefront** into a token-lean, **agent-ready bundle** for rebuilding it on a new stack (e.g. VTEX FastStore v4). It reads the rendered page, so it works **with or without source code**.

```bash
parity migrate --url https://www.loja.com \
  --target faststore --viewports mobile,desktop --open
```

Everything is written to `./parity-migrate/<host>/`.

## Phases (resumable)

1. **Theme + assets** — elects the primary/secondary/background/text colors and the typography / spacing / radii / breakpoint scales; downloads the **logo, favicon, web fonts** (`@font-face`) and an **icon inventory**; a full-page screenshot per viewport.
2. **Sitemap** — discovers and classifies pages, then **samples across kinds** (home, PLPs, PDPs, search, and **institutional** pages) so you capture the whole store — `--sample plp=2,pdp=2,other=3,search=1` (capped at 15).
3. **Components** — per page, each component's rendered HTML, **deterministic Tailwind classes**, interaction hints and **suggested e2e selectors**. Structurally-identical repeats collapse to one representative + a count.

The out dir is **stable per host**, so a re-run skips a completed phase; `--refresh` redoes everything.

## VTEX IO stores

For VTEX IO, `migrate` reads the store's real **block tree** from the render-runtime **on every captured page**, merged and de-duplicated. It writes:

- `blocks.json` — every block **including its `props` — the CMS content the merchant registered** (banner images, texts, links, shelf config). Image references are resolved to absolute URLs.
- `content-assets.json` + `assets/content/` — the content images (block props **and** the Apollo state's product/catalog imagery), **downloaded** locally.
- `component-map.json` — each VTEX block mapped to a target component (`product-summary → ProductCard`), unknowns flagged `custom-component`.

## Output

| File | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `report.html` | **Self-contained visual report** — theme swatches, screenshots, per-page component tables, VTEX → target map. A single file to share. `--open` opens it. |
| `index.md` | The same as token-lean Markdown for an agent. |
| `MIGRATION_PROMPT.md` | Ready-to-paste agent instructions + the target playbook. |
| `custom-theme.scss` | (`--target faststore`) brand tokens → FastStore `--fs-*` global tokens. |
| `manifest.json` | The full raw bundle (HTML + computed styles) — the fallback tier. |

<DocCallout type="warning">
The output contains a real store's content and assets. Keep `parity-migrate/` **gitignored** — never commit scraped store data.
</DocCallout>

## Targets

`--target faststore` appends a playbook (CLI commands, doc links, section/CMS structure, Phosphor icons) and emits `custom-theme.scss`. The capture itself is **target-agnostic** — the same rich data feeds any target (FastStore, TanStack Start, …); only the playbook + theme emitter change.

## See also

- [`parity extract`](/storefront/migration/parity/extract) — the lower-level component snapshot
- [Migration overview](/storefront/migration/overview) · [`@decocms/parity`](https://github.com/decocms/parity)

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/ci
Document: CI/CD & workflows

# CI/CD & workflows

> Gate PRs with parity in GitHub Actions, and drive an AI fix loop from the report.

## PR comment — `parity pr`

`parity pr` wraps `run` (preset `ci`) and emits a Markdown comment with the verdict, top issues, and a link to the full report:

```bash
parity pr --prod https://old.com --preview https://pr-123.preview.dev --github
```

`--github` writes to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` and emits annotations. Drop the output into a PR with `gh pr comment -F` or `actions/github-script`.

## GitHub Actions

```yaml
name: parity
on: pull_request
jobs:
  parity:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: oven-sh/setup-bun@v2
      - run: npx @decocms/parity journey \
          --prod https://old.com \
          --cand ${{ steps.deploy.outputs.preview-url }} \
          --junit results.xml --github
        env:
          ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }}
```

- **Tightest loop:** [`parity journey --junit --github`](/storefront/migration/parity/journey) — only the purchase journey, JUnit + annotations, no LLM aggregation.
- **Full audit:** `parity run --preset ci` (or `parity pr`) for the ranked report.
- **Smoke after deploy:** `parity run --preset smoke` (~30s, no LLM).

<DocCallout type="info">
Exit codes: `--fail-on` (default `critical`) gates the exit. Any blocking issue exits `1`, so the job fails the PR.
</DocCallout>

## The AI fix loop

The report is designed to be driven by an AI coding agent:

1. `parity run --preset full` → read `report.json`.
2. For each `topIssue` with severity ≥ high, open the file(s) in `suggestedFix`.
3. For visual diffs, read `visualDiff.results[].sectionsOnlyInProd` — sections present in prod's DOM but missing in cand (usually not registered in the new site).
4. Fix the candidate, then re-run [`parity journey`](/storefront/migration/parity/journey) (cheaper) to confirm.

<DocCallout type="warning">
prod is the source of truth — never change prod to make a check pass; always fix cand.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [`parity journey`](/storefront/migration/parity/journey) · [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run)
- [Configuration](/storefront/migration/parity/config) — CI env vars

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/performance
Document: Performance (vitals & cache)

# Performance (vitals & cache)

> Compare Web Vitals and cache coverage across many pages, prod vs cand.

These commands focus on performance — lighter than a full `run` (no flows).

## `parity benchmark` — User Navigation Benchmark

A client-facing **before/after** story for a Fresh→TanStack migration. It simulates a real returning visitor and times each step of a shopping journey on **both** sites, producing a **single self-contained HTML** report (PT/EN toggle, mobile/desktop tabs, per-page pagination, scrollable full-page prints, links to every tested page):

```bash
parity benchmark --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev
parity benchmark --prod ... --cand ... --plp /novidades --viewports mobile,desktop --open
```

A **scout** first validates, in the browser, a category (PLP) and a product (PDP) that actually work on **both** sites (render products, no error) and pins the same pages for both. Then, as a returning visitor, each side warms the edge **and the browser cache** (`--warmup-runs`, default 2) and is measured `--measured-runs` times (default 3, median). Each navigation is timed by **wall-clock from the click to the first product image rendering** — not `networkidle` (ad-tracker noise) and not the HAR. Steps: home load, home→PLP (hamburger menu), pagination (per page × per technology), PDP entry, a product→product **SPA shelf hop**, and — only when the product has a colour variant — a variant switch. **Lighthouse** Web Vitals (cold first visit) are added per viewport, and a full HAR of the session is saved per side.

- `--viewports mobile,desktop` (default both) · `--warmup-runs` (2) · `--measured-runs` (3, median)
- `--paginations` (3) · `--plp <path>` (pin the PLP, skip discovery) · `--no-vitals` (skip Lighthouse)
- `--lang pt|en` · `--open` · `--no-auto-selectors`

Steps that break (a route the candidate lacks, pagination that didn't advance) are flagged red and listed in a warning banner instead of being reported as a fake win.

## `parity vitals` — Web Vitals

Crawls N pages (from the sitemap or `--urls`) and compares **LCP, FCP, TTFB, INP, CLS** between prod and cand:

```bash
parity vitals --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev --limit 20 --runs 3
```

- `--limit` — how many pages to sample (default 20)
- `--runs` — repeats per page; the **median** is used (default 1)
- `--viewports` (default `mobile`), `--concurrency` (default 4)

Writes an HTML report with the worst/best regressions expanded. (You can also get the vitals module inside a full run with `parity run --only vitals`.)

## `parity cache` — cache coverage

Crawls N cand pages (no screenshots/vitals — fast) and groups requests by category, surfacing **MISS assets** that should be cached:

```bash
parity cache --cand https://new.dev --pages 30 --cand-only
```

- `--pages` (default 30), `--urls` (explicit list), `--concurrency` (default 6)
- `--cand-only` — skip the prod comparison

Writes an HTML report grouping requests + the caching opportunities.

<DocCallout type="info">
For a single page's deep Lighthouse-style profile, use Chrome DevTools / Lighthouse directly — `parity` focuses on **prod × cand deltas across many pages**.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [`parity run --only vitals` / `--only cache`](/storefront/migration/parity/run#focused-runs)
- [Checks reference](/storefront/migration/parity/checks)

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/pixel-perfect
Document: Pixel-perfect (fix & section)

# Pixel-perfect (fix & section)

> Diff one section between prod and cand — screenshots, pixelmatch heatmap, computed styles — into an LLM-ready bundle.

When a **single section** renders differently, `parity fix` and `parity section` capture everything an agent needs to make it pixel-perfect.

## `parity fix` — the shortcut

`fix` runs `section` with **all signals on** and produces an LLM-ready Markdown bundle:

```bash
parity fix --prod https://old.com --cand https://new.dev --selector "[data-section='Hero']"
```

Writes to `parity-output/`:

- `section-<hash>-prod.png` / `-cand.png` — cropped screenshots of the section on each side
- `section-<hash>-heatmap.png` — **pixelmatch** overlay of the differing pixels
- `section-<hash>-bundle.json` — HTML diff + computed-style diff + asset lists
- `section-<hash>-prompt.md` — a ready-to-paste prompt
- an LLM paragraph diagnosing the difference (with an API key; `--no-llm` skips it)

## `parity section` — pick your facets

`section` is the lower-level command — capture exactly the facets you want:

```bash
parity section --prod ... --cand ... --selector "footer" \
  --screenshot --computed-styles --heatmap --css-source --prompt
```

| Flag | What it adds |
| --- | --- |
| `--screenshot` | Per-side cropped screenshot |
| `--computed-styles` | Computed-style diff (curated ~35 properties) |
| `--heatmap` | pixelmatch bounding-box + hotspots of the pixel diff |
| `--css-source` | Resolves which CSS **rules** produce the styles (via CDP) |
| `--output-html` | HTML diff of the section |
| `--prompt` / `--llm-summary` | Emits `bundle.json` + `prompt.md` / an LLM paragraph |

## Inspecting CSS rules — `parity css-trace`

To see **which CSS rules** affect an element (single URL) or how computed styles differ between the two sides:

```bash
# Every matched rule for a selector on one URL
parity css-trace --url https://loja.com --selector ".price"

# Computed-style diff prod vs cand
parity css-trace --prod ... --cand ... --selector ".price" --json
```

## See also

- [`parity run --only visual`](/storefront/migration/parity/run#focused-runs) — the whole-page visual module
- [Inspect commands](/storefront/migration/parity/commands) — `css-trace`, `html`, `console`

---

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/migration/parity/single-site
Document: Single-site (e2e & audit)

# Single-site (e2e & audit)

> Validate ONE live URL end-to-end — no prod baseline. For pre-launch and partner audits.

When you have **one URL** and want to know "does it actually work?" (not compare it to another), use `e2e` or `audit`.

## `parity e2e` — functional end-to-end

Runs the same flows + checks as `run`, but in **single-site mode** (checks adapt with absolute criteria when there's no other side). It does the same selector automation as `run` — platform detection, grounded LLM selector discovery + live validation, and learning — so you rarely hand-write selectors.

```bash
parity e2e --url https://loja.com --flows search,cart-interactions,login
```

- `--flows` (default `homepage,plp,pdp,purchase-journey,search,cart-interactions`)
- `--search-terms`, `--login-email` / `--login-password`
- `--fail-on critical,high`

Great for **pre-launch** or a **partner audit** — the verdict is on the site's own terms.

## `parity audit` — absolute audit

Lighter than `e2e` (no flows): runs the absolute checks — console, Web Vitals, network, images, SEO — on the given pages:

```bash
parity audit --url https://loja.com --pages "/,/categoria,/p/produto"
```

<DocCallout type="warning">
Don't fake single-site with `parity run --prod X --cand X` — it runs everything twice and reports a degenerate self-diff. `run` rejects a missing `--prod` and points you here.
</DocCallout>

## See also

- [`parity run`](/storefront/migration/parity/run) — the two-URL validator
- [Configuration](/storefront/migration/parity/config) — selectors, flows, credentials

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/hosting/overview
Document: Hosting and deployment [IN BUILD]

# Hosting and deployment

> Deploy your storefront, manage previews, and distinguish site hosting from Studio hosting

> IN BUILD: availability depends on the current implementation and project configuration.

A storefront runtime and the Studio application are separate deployments. Blocks runs with your site's framework; Studio supplies the workspace and Site Editor used to operate it.

Fresh and the former deco.cx admin are retired. Use released Blocks 7.x for the website runtime and Studio Site Editor for current content work.

## Deploy a Blocks storefront

Choose the guide for your current framework:

- **TanStack Start on Cloudflare Workers:** [Production guide](/storefront/blocks/v7/tanstack) and [Worker configuration](/storefront/blocks/v7/deployment/cloudflare-workers).
- **Next.js:** [Next.js integration](/storefront/blocks/v7/nextjs).
- **Existing Fresh sites:** retain the [Fresh runtime documentation](/storefront/blocks/fresh/concepts/block) while planning your [migration](/storefront/migration/overview).

Use a deployment preview to review code changes before production. Content publication is a separate workflow; follow [Releases and publishing](/storefront/editor/content/releases) for the current Site Editor.

## Hosting controls in Studio

<Callout type="warning">
The hosting management surface in Studio is still rolling out behind a feature flag. An existing storefront runtime can be in production while its new management panel is unavailable. Do not assume every organization has the same hosting controls.
</Callout>

Keep application deployment, content publishing, and DNS configuration explicit in your operational workflow. The archived [custom domain](/storefront/legacy/admin/management/custom-domains) and [apex domain](/storefront/legacy/admin/management/apex-domains) guides describe the former admin; they are historical references, not instructions for the new Studio panel.

## Run Studio yourself

Hosting Studio is independent of hosting your storefront. Use [Studio self-hosting](/studio/self-hosting/quickstart) for the platform, and the framework guides above for your site's runtime.

For the transition from the former admin, consult [Legacy admin migration](/storefront/legacy/overview). For hosting controls not yet documented, ask in [Discord](https://decocms.com/discord).

Source: https://docs.decocms.com/storefront/blocks/v7/framework/sdk-utilities
Document: SDK utilities

# SDK utilities

> Verified SDK examples for encoding, redirect maps, server timing and connection-closed retries.

These examples supplement [Packages and exports](/storefront/blocks/v7/packages) and were verified against installed `@decocms/blocks@7.43.0` and the PR #585 source snapshot. For components, scripts and device detection, use [Component patterns](/storefront/blocks/v7/components).

## Base64 encoding

`encodeBase64` accepts a string, `ArrayBuffer`, or `Uint8Array`; `decodeBase64` returns bytes. Decode the bytes with `TextDecoder` when you need text.

```ts

const encoded = encodeBase64("storefront");
const text = new TextDecoder().decode(decodeBase64(encoded));
```

## Redirect maps

`parseRedirectsCsv` reads comma-separated `from,to[,type]` rows. `addRedirects` takes a redirect map and the parsed entries. Build the map from the request's current content so a content reload does not leave a stale map captured at module scope.

```ts

export function findRedirect(pathname: string, blocks: Record<string, unknown>, csv: string) {
  const map = loadRedirects(blocks);
  addRedirects(map, parseRedirectsCsv(csv));
  return matchRedirect(pathname, map);
}
```

The parser uses 301 for `permanent` or `301`, and 302 otherwise. For content-managed CSV redirects, use [the generated-content workflow](/storefront/blocks/v7/content#redirects-from-a-csv-file).

## Server timing

`createServerTimings` creates a collector. `start` returns a completion callback; `record` accepts an already measured duration; `toHeader` serializes the entries.

```ts

export async function timedResponse(run: () => Promise<Response>) {
  const timings = createServerTimings();
  const end = timings.start("upstream");
  const response = await run();
  end();
  const result = new Response(response.body, { status: response.status, headers: response.headers });
  result.headers.set("Server-Timing", timings.toHeader());
  return result;
}
```

## Connection-closed retries

`retryExceptionOr500().execute(fn)` retries errors whose message contains `connection closed before message completed`. It makes at most three attempts, with delays of 100 ms and 200 ms between retries. Despite the helper's name, it does not inspect an HTTP 500 response: a returned response is a successful result. Handle response statuses in application code.

```ts

export function fetchWithConnectionRetry(url: string) {
  return retryExceptionOr500().execute(() => fetch(url));
}
```

Verified modules: [encoding](https://github.com/decocms/blocks/blob/5b37074f3431194f9e48785333b0a99497e81114/packages/blocks/src/sdk/encoding.ts), [redirects](https://github.com/decocms/blocks/blob/5b37074f3431194f9e48785333b0a99497e81114/packages/blocks/src/sdk/redirects.ts), [server timings](https://github.com/decocms/blocks/blob/5b37074f3431194f9e48785333b0a99497e81114/packages/blocks/src/sdk/serverTimings.ts), [retry](https://github.com/decocms/blocks/blob/5b37074f3431194f9e48785333b0a99497e81114/packages/blocks/src/sdk/retry.ts). Reviewed 2026-10-05.

Document: How v7 is built

# How v7 is built

> Why v7 ships as separate packages of plain TypeScript source, how runtime state stays single, and which entry points are safe for the browser.

You don't need this page to build a site. It explains the design decisions behind the packages: why they ship source instead of bundles, how the runtime keeps exactly one copy of its state, and why some imports are server-only. Knowing them helps when an error mentions a duplicate registry, `node:async_hooks` in a browser bundle, or an import that works on the server but not in a client component.

<h2 id="separate-packages-one-direction">Separate packages, one direction</h2>

v7 is five framework packages plus the companion apps:

| Package | Role | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `@decocms/blocks` | The runtime: content, resolution, registries, SDK | no other Deco package |
| `@decocms/blocks-admin` | The admin protocol Studio talks to | `blocks` |
| `@decocms/blocks-cli` | Code generation and migration tools | `blocks` |
| `@decocms/tanstack` | The TanStack Start and Cloudflare Workers binding | `blocks`, `blocks-admin`, `blocks-cli` |
| `@decocms/nextjs` | The Next.js App Router binding | `blocks`, `blocks-admin` |

Dependencies point one way. The runtime never imports a binding, and the two bindings never import each other. That keeps the runtime free of framework code (no TanStack or Next.js types in `@decocms/blocks`), so both bindings share one implementation of resolution, caching helpers and the admin protocol.

When a feature needs something from both sides, it's split rather than given a dependency in the wrong direction. Setup is the example you've already met: `createSiteSetup` in `@decocms/blocks/setup` takes the runtime options, and `createAdminSetup` in `@decocms/blocks-admin/setup` takes the admin ones.

<h2 id="source-not-bundles">Source, not bundles</h2>

Each package's `exports` map points every public path at a TypeScript source file, for example `@decocms/blocks/cms` at the `cms` folder's index file. There is no build step in the packages and no `dist/`. Your bundler (Vite, or Next's) compiles the package source together with your own code.

This is deliberate. An earlier single-package version of the framework shipped a bundled build, and the bundler split shared modules into several output files. A module such as the section registry could then exist twice in the same server, and a section registered through one copy was invisible to code reading the other. Shipping source means each module is compiled once, by your bundler, as part of your app.

<h2 id="one-copy-of-the-runtime-s-state">One copy of the runtime's state</h2>

Even with source exports, a module can occasionally be evaluated more than once: some builds load server functions as separate chunks, for example. So the runtime doesn't keep its state in module variables. It keeps it on a single object, `globalThis.__deco`, which every copy of a module reads and writes:

- the section registry, section options and synchronously registered sections,
- commerce loaders, custom matchers and section loaders,
- the layout, cacheable, SEO, eager and deferred section sets,
- the async rendering configuration,
- the loaded decofile and its revision.

Each module creates its keys on first load if they don't exist yet and otherwise uses the ones already there. Setup can therefore run in any module copy and every other copy sees the result. Treat `globalThis.__deco` as private: read and change this state through the exported functions, never directly.

<h2 id="request-scoped-state-and-conditional-exports">Request-scoped state and conditional exports</h2>

`RequestContext` (see [Request context](/storefront/blocks/v7/request-context)) is built on `AsyncLocalStorage` from `node:async_hooks`, which exists on servers but not in browsers. Its storage is published as `@decocms/blocks/sdk/requestContextStorage` with conditional exports:

```json title="@decocms/blocks package.json (excerpt)"
"./sdk/requestContextStorage": {
  "workerd": "./src/sdk/requestContextStorage.ts",
  "node": "./src/sdk/requestContextStorage.ts",
  "browser": "./src/sdk/requestContextStorage.browser.ts",
  "default": "./src/sdk/requestContextStorage.ts"
}
```

A bundler picks the first condition in the list that the build target has. Browser builds get a stub with the same shape that never holds a request. Server builds get the real implementation.

The order matters. A Cloudflare Workers build activates `workerd`, `worker` and `browser` at once. If `browser` came first, a Workers deploy would get the stub, and cookies, abort signals and device detection would silently stop working in production with no build error. That's why `workerd` and `node` are listed before `browser`.

<h2 id="server-only-and-client-safe-entry-points">Server-only and client-safe entry points</h2>

Some entry points pull in server-only modules (`node:async_hooks`, `node:fs/promises`). Importing any export from them in browser code drags the whole module graph along, because modules are evaluated per file, not per export.

| Import | Safe in browser code | Use it for |
|---|---|---|
| `@decocms/blocks/cms` | No | Setup, loaders, resolution, registration: server code. |
| `@decocms/blocks/cms/client` | Yes | Section registry lookups (`getResolvedComponent`, `registerSection`), section loader mixins, schema helpers, `getDeferredTrigger`. |
| `@decocms/blocks/sdk/requestContext` | Yes | Request-scoped state. In the browser every accessor behaves as outside a request. |
| `@decocms/nextjs/routeHandlers` | Server only | The admin route handlers. The package root also exports client components, which a route handler must not import. |
| `@decocms/nextjs` | Mixed | Server pages and layouts. Client component files import only what they render. |

On TanStack Start, `src/setup.ts` imports from `@decocms/blocks/cms` and still runs in the browser, because the router imports it. That works because the Vite plugin from `@decocms/tanstack/vite` replaces server-only modules with small stubs in the client build. Next.js has no such step and rejects those imports in client components, which is why `@decocms/blocks/cms/client` exists. Use it from any Client Component.

<h2 id="next-steps">Next steps</h2>

- [How resolution works](/storefront/blocks/v7/walkthrough): what the runtime does with a page's content, step by step.
- [The Worker request pipeline](/storefront/blocks/v7/request-pipeline): everything `createDecoWorkerEntry` does with a request.
- [Packages and exports](/storefront/blocks/v7/packages): every public import path.
